# 311. EA-IMETA Enterprise Architecture Layer Model

## 311.1 Purpose

The EA-IMETA Enterprise Architecture Layer Model defines the principal architectural layers through which an enterprise shall be understood, designed, governed, implemented, operated, measured, and evolved.

The Layer Model complements the Domain Framework established in Section 1 and the Cross-Domain Integration Framework established in Section 2.

The Domain Framework answers:

WHAT ARCHITECTURAL DOMAINS EXIST?

The Cross-Domain Integration Framework answers:

HOW DO THE DOMAINS RELATE?

The Layer Model answers:

AT WHICH ARCHITECTURAL LEVEL DOES A PARTICULAR
OBJECT, DECISION, CAPABILITY, SERVICE, CONTROL,
OR CHANGE EXIST?

The three models shall therefore operate together.

---

# 312. Layer Model Principle

## 312.1 Fundamental Principle

EA-IMETA shall distinguish between:

STRATEGIC INTENT

BUSINESS DESIGN

INFORMATION AND DIGITAL STRUCTURE

TECHNOLOGY ENABLEMENT

INTELLIGENCE

CONTROL

OPERATIONS

VALUE

EVOLUTION

These layers shall not be interpreted as isolated technical stacks.

They represent different architectural perspectives on the same enterprise.

---

# 313. EA-IMETA Canonical Layer Model

The initial canonical layer model shall consist of:

LAYER 01
PURPOSE AND STRATEGY

LAYER 02
BUSINESS AND OPERATING MODEL

LAYER 03
CAPABILITY AND VALUE

LAYER 04
PROCESS AND SERVICE

LAYER 05
INFORMATION AND DATA

LAYER 06
APPLICATION AND DIGITAL SERVICES

LAYER 07
TECHNOLOGY AND PLATFORM

LAYER 08
AI AND AGENT INTELLIGENCE

LAYER 09
SECURITY, GOVERNANCE AND CONTROL

LAYER 10
OPERATIONS AND OBSERVABILITY

LAYER 11
ARCHITECTURE INTELLIGENCE

LAYER 12
TRANSFORMATION AND EVOLUTION

These layers shall be connected through the common EA-IMETA meta-model.

---

# 314. Layer Relationships

The canonical relationship shall be:

PURPOSE
   |
   v
STRATEGY
   |
   v
BUSINESS MODEL
   |
   v
CAPABILITY
   |
   v
VALUE STREAM
   |
   v
PROCESS
   |
   v
SERVICE
   |
   v
INFORMATION
   |
   v
DATA
   |
   v
APPLICATION
   |
   v
PLATFORM
   |
   v
TECHNOLOGY
   |
   v
AI / AGENTS
   |
   v
OPERATIONS
   |
   v
OBSERVATION
   |
   v
INTELLIGENCE
   |
   v
EVOLUTION

Security, governance, risk, compliance, resilience, and control operate across all layers.

---

# 315. Layer 01 – Purpose and Strategy

## 315.1 Definition

The Purpose and Strategy Layer establishes the fundamental direction of the enterprise.

It answers:

WHY DOES THE ENTERPRISE EXIST?

WHAT DOES IT SEEK TO ACHIEVE?

WHERE DOES IT WANT TO GO?

WHAT CHOICES WILL IT MAKE?

---

## 315.2 Layer Components

The layer shall include:

Purpose

Vision

Mission

Strategic Intent

Strategic Themes

Strategic Objectives

Strategic Outcomes

Strategic Risks

Strategic Priorities

Strategic Investment Direction

---

## 315.3 Strategic Layer Relationships

PURPOSE
   |
   v
VISION
   |
   v
STRATEGIC INTENT
   |
   v
OBJECTIVES
   |
   v
OUTCOMES
   |
   v
PRIORITIES

---

# 316. Layer 02 – Business and Operating Model

## 316.1 Definition

The Business and Operating Model Layer describes how the enterprise is structured to create and deliver value.

It translates strategy into an executable enterprise model.

---

## 316.2 Layer Components

The layer shall include:

Business Model

Operating Model

Business Functions

Organization

Business Relationships

Business Services

Sourcing Model

Decision Model

STRATEGY
   |
   v

   |
   v
OPERATING MODEL
   |
   v
ORGANIZATION
   |
   v
EXECUTION

---

# 317. Layer 03 – Capability and Value

## 317.1 Definition

The Capability and Value Layer describes what the enterprise must be able to do and how those capabilities contribute to stakeholder value.

Capability is the primary architectural abstraction in this layer.

---

## 317.2 Layer Components

The layer shall include:

Capabilities

Capability Groups

Capability Hierarchies

Capability Maturity

Value Streams

Value Stages

Business Outcomes

Benefits

Value Measures

---

## 317.3 Capability Principle

Capabilities shall describe:

WHAT THE ENTERPRISE CAN DO

rather than:

HOW THE ENTERPRISE DOES IT.

---

## 317.4 Capability Relationship

STRATEGY
   |
   v
CAPABILITY
   |
   +-------------------+
   |                   |
   v                   v
VALUE STREAM       BUSINESS OUTCOME
   |                   |
   +---------+---------+
             |
             v
            VALUE

# 318. Layer 04 – Process and Service

## 318.1 Definition

The Process and Service Layer describes how capabilities are operationalized through processes and services.

---

## 318.2 Layer Components

Tasks

## 318.4 Service Principle

A service shall represent a defined capability or function made available to a consumer.

Services may be:

Business Services

Application Services

Technology Services

AI Services

Platform Services

# 319. Layer 05 – Information and Data

## 319.1 Definition

The Information and Data Layer describes the semantic and technical representation of enterprise information.

The layer connects business meaning to machine-readable data.

## 319.2 Information Layer

Information represents:

Meaning

Context

Concepts

Relationships

Business Interpretation

---

## 319.3 Data Layer

Data represents:

Records

Values

Events

Measurements

Transactions

Observations

Machine-Readable Structures

## 319.4 Layer Relationship

BUSINESS CONCEPT
      |
      v

      |
      v
DATA ENTITY
      |
      v
DATA STORE
      |
      v
DATA PRODUCT

# 320. Information Semantics

Information Architecture shall establish semantic meaning.

For example:

CUSTOMER

may represent a business concept.

The data architecture may then represent:

Customer ID

Customer Name

Customer Address

Customer Status

Customer Transaction

The semantic relationship shall remain explicit.

# 344. Architecture Intelligence Questions

The intelligence layer should support questions such as:

What capabilities are affected by this application?

What applications depend on this technology?

Which data assets support this capability?

What risks arise from this architecture change?

Which agents have authority over this process?

What investments support this strategic objective?

What architecture debt is increasing?

What architecture changes would improve resilience?

## 345.1 Definition

The Transformation and Evolution Layer manages movement from current architecture to desired future architecture.

It provides the mechanism through which architecture becomes change.

Programs

Projects

Outcomes

      v

When It Changes

Who Owns It

What Dependencies Exist

What Investment Is Required

What Risks Exist

What Benefits Are Expected

---

# 348. Architecture Evolution Cycle

Architecture shall evolve continuously.

OBSERVE
   |
   v
UNDERSTAND
   |
   v
ASSESS
   |
   v
DESIGN
   |
   v
DECIDE
   |
   v
IMPLEMENT
   |
   v
MEASURE
   |
   v
LEARN
   |
   v
EVOLVE

---

# 349. Layer Interaction Model

The complete EA-IMETA Layer Model shall operate as:

                    PURPOSE / STRATEGY
                            |
                            v
                 BUSINESS / OPERATING MODEL
                            |
                            v
                     CAPABILITY / VALUE
                            |
                            v
                     PROCESS / SERVICE
                            |
                            v
                    INFORMATION / DATA
                            |
                            v
                APPLICATION / DIGITAL SERVICE
                            |
                            v
                   TECHNOLOGY / PLATFORM
                            |
                            v
                     AI / AGENT LAYER
                            |
                            v
                     OPERATIONS LAYER
                            |
                            v
                  OBSERVATION / EVIDENCE
                            |
                            v
                  ARCHITECTURE INTELLIGENCE
                            |
                            v
                    TRANSFORMATION
                            |
                            v
                         EVOLUTION

Cross-cutting:

SECURITY

GOVERNANCE

RISK

COMPLIANCE

RESILIENCE

operate across the complete model.

---

# 350. Layer Dependency Principle

Each lower layer shall be traceable to the higher-level purpose it supports.

For example:

Technology shall support Applications.

Applications shall support Capabilities.

Capabilities shall support Strategy.

This shall prevent technology architecture from becoming detached from enterprise purpose.

---

# 351. Reverse Traceability

Traceability shall also work upward.

For example:

Technology

↓

Application

↓

Capability

↓

Strategic Objective

↓

Purpose

This enables the enterprise to answer:

WHY DO WE HAVE THIS TECHNOLOGY?

---

# 352. Layer Ownership

Each layer shall have defined architectural ownership.

Ownership may be distributed across:

Enterprise Architect

Business Architect

Capability Architect

Information Architect

Data Architect

Application Architect

Technology Architect

AI Architect

Agent Architect

Security Architect

Operations Architect

Transformation Architect

---

# 353. Layer Governance

Layer governance shall ensure:

Consistent Definitions

Clear Ownership

Traceability

Cross-Layer Dependencies

Architecture Decisions

Standards

Security

Risk

Lifecycle Management

---

# 354. Layer Transition Rules

Architecture objects may move through lifecycle states.

For example:

CONCEPT

↓

DESIGNED

↓

APPROVED

↓

IMPLEMENTED

↓

OPERATIONAL

↓

OPTIMIZED

↓

RETIRED

The lifecycle state shall remain independent of the architectural layer.

---

# 355. Layer and Lifecycle Relationship

The architecture shall therefore represent two dimensions:

ARCHITECTURAL LAYER

and

LIFECYCLE STATE

For example:

APPLICATION
    |
    +-- Concept
    +-- Designed
    +-- Approved
    +-- Implemented
    +-- Operational
    +-- Retired

This allows architecture repositories to represent both structure and change.

---

# 356. Layer and Domain Relationship

Domains and layers shall not be treated as identical.

A domain represents:

WHO / WHAT SPECIALIST AREA

A layer represents:

AT WHAT ARCHITECTURAL LEVEL

Therefore:

AI is a domain.

AI capabilities may operate across several layers.

Security is a domain.

Security controls may operate across all layers.

Data is a domain.

Data objects primarily operate within the Information and Data Layer but may have relationships across the architecture.

---

# 357. Domain-Layer Matrix

A future EA-IMETA repository may maintain a domain-layer matrix.

Example:

| Domain | Primary Layer | Secondary Layers |
|---|---|---|
| Strategy | Purpose / Strategy | Capability |
| Business | Business / Operating Model | Capability, Process |
| Capability | Capability / Value | Strategy, Process |
| Process | Process / Service | Capability, Operations |
| Information | Information / Data | Business, Process |
| Data | Information / Data | Application, Technology |
| Application | Application / Digital | Process, Data, Technology |
| Technology | Technology / Platform | Application, Operations |
| AI | AI / Agent | Data, Application, Operations |
| Agent | AI / Agent | Process, Application, Operations |
| Security | Cross-Cutting | All Layers |
| Governance | Cross-Cutting | All Layers |
| Operations | Operations | Technology, Application, Business |
| Intelligence | Intelligence | All Layers |
| Transformation | Transformation | All Layers |

---

# 358. Layer Integration Metrics

The quality of the Layer Model shall be measured through:

Layer Traceability

Cross-Layer Relationship Coverage

Orphan Object Rate

Architecture Drift

Layer Conflict Rate

Requirement Coverage

Implementation Traceability

Operational Alignment

---

# 359. Layer Anti-Patterns

The following shall be avoided.

## 359.1 Layer Isolation

Treating each layer as independent.

---

## 359.2 Technology Dominance

Allowing technology to define enterprise architecture rather than support it.

---

## 359.3 Missing Business Traceability

Implementing systems without clear business purpose.

---

## 359.4 Missing Operational Feedback

Failing to update architecture based on operational reality.

---

## 359.5 Intelligence Without Governance

Allowing AI or analytics to influence architecture without defined authority and accountability.

---

## 359.6 Architecture Without Evolution

Treating architecture as a static document rather than a living system.

---

# 360. Layer Maturity

The Layer Model shall mature through:

LEVEL 1
DOCUMENTED

LEVEL 2
STRUCTURED

LEVEL 3
CONNECTED

LEVEL 4
INTEGRATED

LEVEL 5
DYNAMIC

LEVEL 6
INTELLIGENT

---

# 361. Layer Intelligence

At advanced maturity levels, architecture intelligence shall be capable of analyzing relationships between layers.

Examples:

A technology failure may affect:

Technology

Application

Service

Process

Capability

Business Outcome

Value

Similarly:

A strategic change may propagate downward through:

Strategy

Capability

Process

Application

Data

Technology

Operations

---

# 362. Layer Impact Analysis

A change in any layer shall potentially trigger analysis of dependent layers.

Example:

TECHNOLOGY CHANGE
       |
       v
APPLICATION IMPACT
       |
       v
SERVICE IMPACT
       |
       v
PROCESS IMPACT
       |
       v
CAPABILITY IMPACT
       |
       v
BUSINESS IMPACT
       |
       v
VALUE IMPACT

The reverse direction shall also be supported.

---

# 363. Layer Simulation

The Digital Twin and Simulation capability may use the Layer Model to simulate:

Technology Failure

Application Failure

Process Change

Capability Change

Demand Increase

AI Adoption

Agent Deployment

Security Incident

Regulatory Change

Investment Change

---

# 364. Layer-Based Architecture Scenarios

Scenario analysis may evaluate:

What happens if a critical application fails?

What happens if a capability is removed?

What happens if AI replaces a process activity?

What happens if an agent receives additional authority?

What happens if a technology platform becomes unavailable?

What happens if a regulation changes?

The answers shall be derived through architecture relationships.

---

# 365. Layer-Based Architecture Optimization

Optimization shall seek improvements across multiple layers.

For example:

A technology modernization may improve:

Technology Cost

Application Performance

Process Efficiency

Capability Performance

Business Outcome

Value

Therefore optimization shall not be limited to the layer where the change originates.

---

# 366. Layer-Based Architecture Decision

Every material architecture decision should identify:

Originating Layer

Affected Layers

Affected Domains

Dependencies

Risks

Benefits

Value

Implementation

Operational Consequences

Review Conditions

---

# 367. Layer-Based Architecture Repository

The repository shall allow objects to be queried by:

Domain

Layer

Lifecycle

Owner

Status

Version

Relationship

Dependency

Risk

Value

---

# 368. Layer-Based Knowledge Graph

The Knowledge Graph shall allow relationships such as:

Strategy → Capability

Capability → Process

Process → Service

Service → Application

Application → Technology

Data → Application

AI → Decision

Agent → Action

Risk → Control

Implementation → Architecture

Operation → Architecture

Outcome → Value

---

# 369. Layer-Based Architecture Intelligence

Architecture Intelligence shall be capable of reasoning across layers.

For example:

QUESTION

Which strategic objectives are affected
by the retirement of Technology X?

TRACE

Technology X
    ↓
Applications
    ↓
Services
    ↓
Processes
    ↓
Capabilities
    ↓
Strategic Objectives

This is a core example of EA-IMETA architecture intelligence.

---

# 370. Layer-Based Governance

Governance shall evaluate whether changes are appropriate across all affected layers.

A seemingly technical change may have:

Business Consequences

Security Consequences

Operational Consequences

Financial Consequences

Strategic Consequences

Therefore governance shall be cross-layer.

---

# 371. Layer-Based Transformation

Transformation initiatives shall use the Layer Model to identify complete change scope.

For example:

DIGITAL TRANSFORMATION

may affect:

Strategy

Business Model

Capabilities

Processes

Information

Data

Applications

Technology

AI

Operations

Security

Governance

Value

---

# 372. Layer-Based Architecture Roadmap

Roadmaps should allow change to be visualized across layers.

                 CURRENT        TRANSITION       TARGET

STRATEGY             X--------------X--------------X

CAPABILITY           X---------X----X--------------X

PROCESS              X---------X---------X--------X

DATA                 X-------------X--------------X

APPLICATION          X------X-------------X------X

TECHNOLOGY           X---X----------------X------X

AI                   --------X-----------X--------X

OPERATIONS           X------X------X-------------X

The roadmap shall remain connected to architecture objects and dependencies.

---

# 373. Layer Model and Enterprise Value

The ultimate purpose of the Layer Model is not technical completeness.

It is enterprise value.

The relationship shall therefore remain:

PURPOSE
   |
   v
STRATEGY
   |
   v
ARCHITECTURE
   |
   v
CAPABILITY
   |
   v
EXECUTION
   |
   v
OUTCOME
   |
   v
VALUE

---

# 374. Section 3 Summary

EA-IMETA-MASTER-01 Part 2 Section 3 establishes the Enterprise Architecture Layer Model.

The Layer Model defines the principal architectural levels through which the enterprise shall be understood.

It establishes:

Purpose and Strategy

Business and Operating Model

Capability and Value

Process and Service

Information and Data

Application and Digital Services

Technology and Platform

AI and Agent Intelligence

Security, Governance and Control

Operations and Observability

Architecture Intelligence

Transformation and Evolution

The fundamental principle is:

AN ENTERPRISE ARCHITECTURE IS NOT ONLY A SET
OF DOMAINS.

IT IS ALSO A SET OF CONNECTED ARCHITECTURAL
LAYERS THROUGH WHICH VALUE, INFORMATION,
DECISIONS, TECHNOLOGY, CONTROL AND CHANGE FLOW.

The Layer Model therefore provides a second major structural dimension to EA-IMETA.

Together:

DOMAIN MODEL

+

LAYER MODEL

+

CROSS-DOMAIN RELATIONSHIPS

+

TRACEABILITY

+

GOVERNANCE

form the structural basis for the complete EA-IMETA enterprise architecture system.

---

# 375. EA-IMETA Architecture Building Block Framework

## 375.1 Purpose

The EA-IMETA Architecture Building Block Framework defines the reusable structural units from which the enterprise architecture shall be designed, described, governed, implemented, and evolved.

The framework establishes a common mechanism for identifying:

Architecture Building Blocks

Business Building Blocks

Solution Building Blocks

Technology Building Blocks

Data Building Blocks

AI Building Blocks

Agent Building Blocks

Security Building Blocks

Governance Building Blocks

Operational Building Blocks

Transformation Building Blocks

The objective is to move EA-IMETA from architecture descriptions toward reusable architecture components.

---

# 376. Architecture Building Block Principle

## 376.1 Fundamental Principle

An Architecture Building Block shall represent a reusable architectural capability, structure, service, pattern, component, control, or design element.

The fundamental principle is:

DESIGN ONCE

DEFINE CLEARLY

GOVERN CENTRALLY

REUSE WHERE APPROPRIATE

EVOLVE CONTINUOUSLY

---

# 377. Building Block Definition

An Architecture Building Block shall contain sufficient information to establish:

What it is

Why it exists

What capability it provides

Where it applies

What it depends on

What depends on it

Who owns it

How it is governed

How it is implemented

How it is measured

How it evolves

---

# 378. Building Block Categories

EA-IMETA shall distinguish between the following primary categories:

01 Business Building Blocks

02 Capability Building Blocks

03 Process Building Blocks

04 Information Building Blocks

05 Data Building Blocks

06 Application Building Blocks

07 Technology Building Blocks

08 Platform Building Blocks

09 AI Building Blocks

10 Agent Building Blocks

11 Security Building Blocks

12 Governance Building Blocks

13 Risk and Compliance Building Blocks

14 Operational Building Blocks

15 Integration Building Blocks

16 Architecture Intelligence Building Blocks

17 Digital Twin Building Blocks

18 Transformation Building Blocks

19 Value Building Blocks

---

# 379. Business Building Blocks

## 379.1 Definition

Business Building Blocks represent reusable business structures required to support enterprise operations.

Examples include:

Business Function

Business Service

Business Role

Business Unit

Business Policy

Business Capability

Business Rule

Business Process

---

## 379.2 Business Building Block Relationship

BUSINESS OBJECTIVE
        |
        v
BUSINESS CAPABILITY
        |
        v
BUSINESS SERVICE
        |
        v
BUSINESS PROCESS
        |
        v
BUSINESS OUTCOME

---

# 380. Capability Building Blocks

## 380.1 Definition

Capability Building Blocks represent reusable capability structures.

A capability building block may include:

Capability Definition

Capability Owner

Capability Maturity

Capability Requirements

Supporting Processes

Supporting Applications

Supporting Data

Supporting Technology

Supporting People

Performance Measures

---

# 381. Capability Building Block Principle

Capabilities shall remain independent from their implementation.

Therefore a capability building block may be realized through different combinations of:

People

Process

Information

Data

Applications

Technology

AI

Agents

---

# 382. Process Building Blocks

## 382.1 Definition

Process Building Blocks represent reusable process structures.

They may contain:

Process Definition

Inputs

Outputs

Activities

Roles

Rules

Systems

Data

Controls

Measures

---

## 382.2 Process Reuse

Reusable process patterns may be applied across business units where:

Purpose is equivalent

Control requirements are equivalent

Outcome requirements are equivalent

Local variation remains governed

---

# 383. Information Building Blocks

## 383.1 Definition

Information Building Blocks represent reusable semantic structures.

Examples include:

Business Concepts

Information Objects

Information Relationships

Information Classifications

Information Flows

Information Domains

---

# 384. Data Building Blocks

## 384.1 Definition

Data Building Blocks represent reusable data structures and services.

Examples include:

Data Entity

Data Product

Data Service

Data Store

Data Pipeline

Data Quality Rule

Data Classification

Data Governance Rule

---

# 385. Data Building Block Lifecycle

DEFINE
  |
  v
MODEL
  |
  v
GOVERN
  |
  v
IMPLEMENT
  |
  v
USE
  |
  v
MONITOR
  |
  v
IMPROVE
  |
  v
RETIRE

---

# 386. Application Building Blocks

## 386.1 Definition

Application Building Blocks represent reusable software structures or application services.

Examples include:

Application Service

API

Workflow Engine

Authentication Service

Document Service

Notification Service

Search Service

Integration Service

Reporting Service

---

# 387. Application Building Block Principle

Applications should preferentially consume reusable enterprise services rather than independently recreating common capabilities.

This principle supports:

Reuse

Consistency

Security

Maintainability

Cost Efficiency

---

# 388. Technology Building Blocks

## 388.1 Definition

Technology Building Blocks represent reusable technical components.

Examples include:

Compute

Storage

Network

Runtime

Database

Operating System

Container

Virtual Machine

Infrastructure Service

---

# 389. Platform Building Blocks

## 389.1 Definition

Platform Building Blocks provide reusable technical foundations upon which applications and services may be built.

Examples include:

Cloud Platform

Data Platform

Integration Platform

Developer Platform

AI Platform

Identity Platform

Observability Platform

---

# 390. AI Building Blocks

## 390.1 Definition

AI Building Blocks represent reusable artificial intelligence capabilities.

Examples include:

AI Model

Model Service

Embedding Service

Retrieval Service

Evaluation Service

Prompt Service

AI Gateway

AI Safety Service

AI Monitoring

---

# 391. AI Building Block Governance

Each AI Building Block shall have:

Owner

Purpose

Model

Version

Data Requirements

Security Classification

Risk Classification

Performance Criteria

Evaluation Method

Lifecycle

---

# 392. Agent Building Blocks

## 392.1 Definition

Agent Building Blocks represent reusable structures required to create governed enterprise agents.

Examples include:

Agent Identity

Agent Role

Agent Objective

Agent Memory

Agent Tool

Agent Policy

Agent Permission

Agent Workflow

Agent Supervisor

Agent Evaluation

---

# 393. Agent Building Block Model

AGENT IDENTITY
      |
      v
AGENT ROLE
      |
      v
AGENT OBJECTIVE
      |
      v
AGENT POLICY
      |
      v
AGENT TOOLS
      |
      v
AGENT ACTION
      |
      v
AGENT RESULT
      |
      v
AGENT EVALUATION

---

# 394. Security Building Blocks

## 394.1 Definition

Security Building Blocks represent reusable security capabilities and controls.

Examples include:

Identity Provider

Authentication

Authorization

Encryption

Secrets Management

Security Monitoring

Threat Detection

Vulnerability Management

Security Incident Response

---

# 395. Security Control Building Blocks

Security controls shall be reusable where appropriate.

Each control should define:

Control Objective

Threat Addressed

Risk Addressed

Implementation

Owner

Evidence

Test

Frequency

Status

---

# 396. Governance Building Blocks

## 396.1 Definition

Governance Building Blocks represent reusable governance mechanisms.

Examples include:

Policy

Standard

Decision Authority

Approval Process

Architecture Review

Exception Process

Assurance Process

Audit Mechanism

---

# 397. Risk and Compliance Building Blocks

Examples include:

Risk Assessment

Control Assessment

Compliance Requirement

Regulatory Obligation

Evidence Record

Compliance Test

Risk Treatment

---

# 398. Operational Building Blocks

Operational Building Blocks represent reusable structures for operating enterprise services.

Examples include:

Monitoring

Alerting

Incident Management

Problem Management

Change Management

Service Management

Backup

Recovery

Capacity Management

---

# 399. Integration Building Blocks

## 399.1 Definition

Integration Building Blocks provide reusable mechanisms for connecting architecture components.

Examples include:

API Gateway

Message Broker

Event Bus

Integration Service

Data Pipeline

File Exchange

Event Stream

Service Mesh

---

# 400. Integration Principle

Integration shall preferably use governed reusable patterns rather than point-to-point connections.

Preferred model:

SYSTEM A
    |
    v
INTEGRATION PLATFORM
    |
    +---- SYSTEM B
    |
    +---- SYSTEM C
    |
    +---- SYSTEM D

rather than:

SYSTEM A ---- SYSTEM B
    |             |
    +---- SYSTEM C
    |             |
    +---- SYSTEM D

where the latter creates uncontrolled integration complexity.

---

# 401. Architecture Intelligence Building Blocks

Architecture Intelligence Building Blocks may include:

Architecture Search

Semantic Search

Knowledge Graph

Dependency Engine

Impact Analysis

Architecture Analytics

Recommendation Engine

Architecture Copilot

Conformance Engine

---

# 402. Digital Twin Building Blocks

Digital Twin Building Blocks may include:

Enterprise State Model

Asset Model

Dependency Model

Event Stream

Simulation Engine

Scenario Model

Prediction Engine

Digital Twin Interface

---

# 403. Transformation Building Blocks

Transformation Building Blocks may include:

Transformation Initiative

Migration Work Package

Architecture Roadmap

Transition Architecture

Transformation Capability

Change Dependency

Benefit Realization

---

# 404. Value Building Blocks

Value Building Blocks represent reusable structures for measuring and managing enterprise value.

Examples include:

Benefit

Outcome

Value Driver

Cost

Investment

ROI

Risk-Adjusted Value

Value Metric

---

# 405. Building Block Granularity

Building Blocks shall be defined at an appropriate level of granularity.

Too large:

Enterprise Transformation Platform

Too small:

Individual Database Field

The appropriate level shall allow meaningful reuse and governance.

---

# 406. Building Block Qualification

An object should become a formal Architecture Building Block when it demonstrates:

Reusable Value

Defined Purpose

Defined Ownership

Defined Interfaces

Defined Dependencies

Defined Lifecycle

Defined Governance

---

# 407. Building Block Identity

Each Building Block shall have a unique identifier.

Example:

ABB-BUS-001

ABB-CAP-001

ABB-DAT-001

ABB-APP-001

ABB-TEC-001

ABB-AI-001

ABB-AGT-001

ABB-SEC-001

ABB-GOV-001

ABB-OPS-001

The exact identifier structure shall be governed by the EA-IMETA repository.

---

# 408. Building Block Metadata

A Building Block should contain:

Building Block ID

Name

Type

Description

Purpose

Owner

Domain

Layer

Status

Version

Lifecycle State

Dependencies

Consumers

Interfaces

Standards

Security Classification

Risk Classification

Value Contribution

Evidence

---

# 409. Building Block Lifecycle

Every Building Block shall follow a controlled lifecycle.

PROPOSED
    |
    v
ASSESSED
    |
    v
DESIGNED
    |
    v
APPROVED
    |
    v
PUBLISHED
    |
    v
REUSED
    |
    v
MONITORED
    |
    v
UPDATED
    |
    v
DEPRECATED
    |
    v
RETIRED

---

# 410. Building Block Governance

Building Blocks shall be governed according to their significance.

Low-impact Building Blocks may require domain-level approval.

High-impact Building Blocks may require enterprise architecture approval.

Critical Building Blocks may require executive governance.

---

# 411. Building Block Approval

Approval criteria may include:

Architecture Alignment

Business Value

Security

Risk

Compliance

Technical Feasibility

Operational Readiness

Reuse Potential

Lifecycle Sustainability

---

# 412. Building Block Reuse

Reuse shall be encouraged when it reduces:

Cost

Complexity

Risk

Development Time

Operational Burden

Security Exposure

Architecture Debt

---

# 413. Building Block Composition

Building Blocks may be composed into larger structures.

Example:

CAPABILITY
    |
    +-- Business Building Block
    |
    +-- Process Building Block
    |
    +-- Data Building Block
    |
    +-- Application Building Block
    |
    +-- Technology Building Block
    |
    +-- Security Building Block
    |
    +-- AI Building Block

---

# 414. Composite Architecture Building Blocks

A Composite Building Block may combine multiple lower-level Building Blocks into an integrated architecture capability.

Example:

CUSTOMER MANAGEMENT PLATFORM
        |
        +-- Identity
        +-- Customer Data
        +-- Customer Application
        +-- API Layer
        +-- AI Service
        +-- Security Controls
        +-- Monitoring

---

# 415. Building Block Interfaces

Each reusable Building Block should define clear interfaces.

Interfaces may include:

API

Event

Data Interface

Service Interface

Human Interaction

Agent Interaction

Governance Interface

Operational Interface

---

# 416. Building Block Dependencies

Dependencies shall be explicitly recorded.

Example:

AI SERVICE
   |
   +-- requires --> DATA PLATFORM
   |
   +-- requires --> MODEL
   |
   +-- requires --> SECURITY
   |
   +-- requires --> AI GOVERNANCE
   |
   +-- supports --> BUSINESS SERVICE

---

# 417. Building Block Consumers

The architecture repository should identify which objects consume each Building Block.

For example:

DATA PLATFORM
     |
     +-- consumed by --> Application A
     +-- consumed by --> Application B
     +-- consumed by --> AI Service C
     +-- consumed by --> Agent D

This enables impact analysis.

---

# 418. Building Block Impact Analysis

When a Building Block changes, the architecture should identify:

Consumers

Dependencies

Affected Capabilities

Affected Services

Affected Applications

Affected Data

Affected Technology

Affected Security Controls

Affected AI

Affected Agents

Affected Operations

---

# 419. Building Block Standardization

Common Building Blocks should be standardized where appropriate.

Standardization may include:

Naming

Interfaces

Security

Performance

Data Formats

Operational Requirements

Monitoring

Lifecycle

---

# 420. Building Block Reference Architecture

A Reference Architecture may be assembled from approved Building Blocks.

Example:

REFERENCE ARCHITECTURE
        |
        +-- Capability
        |
        +-- Business Service
        |
        +-- Data Platform
        |
        +-- Application Service
        |
        +-- Integration
        |
        +-- Security
        |
        +-- AI
        |
        +-- Operations

---

# 421. Building Block Patterns

EA-IMETA shall support reusable architecture patterns.

Examples:

API Pattern

Event-Driven Pattern

Data Product Pattern

AI Service Pattern

Agent Pattern

Zero Trust Pattern

Platform Pattern

Observability Pattern

Digital Twin Pattern

---

# 422. Building Block Anti-Patterns

The following shall be avoided:

Duplicate Building Blocks

Unowned Building Blocks

Unmanaged Dependencies

Hidden Interfaces

Uncontrolled Variants

Deprecated Components in Active Use

Local Components Presented as Enterprise Standards

Technology-Specific Components Without Architectural Purpose

---

# 423. Building Block Catalogue

The EA-IMETA repository shall maintain a Building Block Catalogue.

The catalogue should allow users to search by:

Name

Type

Domain

Layer

Capability

Owner

Status

Technology

Security Classification

Lifecycle

Reuse Count

---

# 424. Building Block Certification

Certain Building Blocks may be formally certified.

Certification may indicate:

Enterprise Approved

Domain Approved

Security Approved

Technology Approved

AI Approved

Operationally Approved

Deprecated

---

# 425. Certified Building Block

A certified Building Block shall have:

Certification Authority

Certification Date

Certification Scope

Certification Conditions

Review Date

Evidence

---

# 426. Building Block Versioning

Building Blocks shall be version controlled.

Example:

ABB-APP-001 v1.0

ABB-APP-001 v1.1

ABB-APP-001 v2.0

Major versions may introduce breaking changes.

Minor versions should maintain compatibility where possible.

---

# 427. Building Block Compatibility

Compatibility shall be assessed across:

API

Data

Security

Performance

Dependencies

Operational Requirements

Lifecycle

---

# 428. Building Block Substitution

Where a Building Block is replaced, the architecture shall identify:

Replacement

Migration Path

Compatibility

Dependencies

Risks

Benefits

Retirement Date

---

# 429. Building Block Modernization

Building Blocks shall be candidates for modernization where:

Technology becomes obsolete

Risk increases

Cost becomes excessive

Performance decreases

Security becomes inadequate

Better enterprise standards become available

---

# 430. Building Block Reuse Metrics

Potential metrics include:

Reuse Count

Reuse Rate

Duplicate Reduction

Cost Avoidance

Development Time Reduction

Security Control Reuse

Architecture Debt Reduction

---

# 431. Building Block Economics

Building Block economics shall consider:

Development Cost

Maintenance Cost

Operating Cost

Reuse Benefit

Cost Avoidance

Risk Reduction

Lifecycle Cost

Retirement Cost

---

# 432. Building Block Value

A Building Block is valuable when it contributes to:

Capability

Efficiency

Standardization

Security

Resilience

Reuse

Innovation

Transformation

Enterprise Value

---

# 433. Building Block and Architecture Repository

The Building Block Catalogue shall be integrated with the architecture repository.

The relationship shall be:

ARCHITECTURE REPOSITORY
        |
        +-- Building Blocks
        |
        +-- Domains
        |
        +-- Layers
        |
        +-- Capabilities
        |
        +-- Relationships
        |
        +-- Decisions
        |
        +-- Standards
        |
        +-- Risks

---

# 434. Building Block and Knowledge Graph

Building Blocks shall be represented as nodes in the EA-IMETA Knowledge Graph.

Example:

[CAPABILITY]
      |
      | realized-by
      v
[APPLICATION]
      |
      | runs-on
      v
[PLATFORM]
      |
      | protected-by
      v
[SECURITY CONTROL]

---

# 435. Building Block and Architecture Intelligence

Architecture Intelligence shall use Building Blocks to answer:

Which approved components can realize this capability?

Which Building Blocks are affected by this change?

Which Building Blocks are underused?

Which Building Blocks create excessive dependencies?

Which Building Blocks should be standardized?

Which Building Blocks should be retired?

---

# 436. Building Block Recommendation

AI may recommend Building Blocks based on:

Requirement

Capability

Architecture Pattern

Security

Risk

Technology

Cost

Reuse

The recommendation shall remain subject to architecture governance.

---

# 437. Building Block Selection Process

The selection process shall be:

REQUIREMENT
    |
    v
ARCHITECTURE CONTEXT
    |
    v
SEARCH CATALOGUE
    |
    v
CANDIDATE BUILDING BLOCKS
    |
    v
EVALUATION
    |
    v
SECURITY / RISK REVIEW
    |
    v
ARCHITECTURE DECISION
    |
    v
SELECT
    |
    v
IMPLEMENT

---

# 438. Building Block Design Process

Where no suitable Building Block exists:

REQUIREMENT
    |
    v
GAP IDENTIFICATION
    |
    v
DESIGN NEW BUILDING BLOCK
    |
    v
ASSESS
    |
    v
APPROVE
    |
    v
PUBLISH
    |
    v
REUSE

---

# 439. Building Block Lifecycle Governance

The lifecycle shall include periodic review.

Review criteria:

Still Relevant?

Still Secure?

Still Cost Effective?

Still Compliant?

Still Reusable?

Still Aligned?

Still Supported?

---

# 440. Building Block Retirement

A Building Block may be retired when:

No Longer Needed

Replaced

Obsolete

Unsafe

Non-Compliant

Too Expensive

Unsupported

Architecturally Superseded

---

# 441. Retirement Principle

Retirement shall not mean immediate deletion.

A retired Building Block shall remain historically traceable.

The repository shall preserve:

Identity

Version

Historical Consumers

Replacement

Retirement Decision

Retirement Date

Reason

---

# 442. Building Block Traceability

Every Building Block shall be traceable to:

Purpose

Requirement

Capability

Architecture Decision

Standard

Implementation

Consumer

Risk

Value

---

# 443. Building Block Conformance

Implementations using a Building Block should be evaluated for conformance.

Conformance may include:

Interface Compliance

Security Compliance

Data Compliance

Operational Compliance

Architecture Standard Compliance

Version Compliance

---

# 444. Building Block Exception Management

Where an implementation cannot use the approved Building Block, an exception may be requested.

The exception shall document:

Reason

Alternative

Risk

Cost

Impact

Duration

Owner

Approval

Review Date

---

# 445. Building Block and Technical Debt

Failure to reuse approved Building Blocks may increase architecture debt.

The architecture should therefore monitor:

Duplicate Implementations

Local Variants

Unapproved Components

Unsupported Versions

---

# 446. Building Block and Transformation

Transformation programs should preferentially use approved Building Blocks.

This supports:

Faster Delivery

Reduced Risk

Consistent Architecture

Reuse

Standardization

Lower Lifecycle Cost

---

# 447. Building Block and Digital Twin

Digital Twin models may represent Building Blocks as reusable architecture components.

This allows simulation of:

Component Failure

Replacement

Scaling

Capacity

Dependency

Cost

Risk

---

# 448. Building Block and Autonomous Enterprise

Autonomous enterprise capabilities shall themselves be constructed from governed Building Blocks.

Example:

AUTONOMOUS BUSINESS SERVICE
        |
        +-- AI Building Block
        +-- Agent Building Block
        +-- Data Building Block
        +-- Identity Building Block
        +-- Security Building Block
        +-- Governance Building Block
        +-- Monitoring Building Block

---

# 449. Building Block Maturity

Building Block capability shall mature through:

LEVEL 1
AD HOC COMPONENTS

LEVEL 2
IDENTIFIED COMPONENTS

LEVEL 3
CATALOGUED BUILDING BLOCKS

LEVEL 4
GOVERNED REUSABLE BUILDING BLOCKS

LEVEL 5
INTELLIGENTLY COMPOSED BUILDING BLOCKS

LEVEL 6
DYNAMICALLY OPTIMIZED ARCHITECTURE COMPONENTS

---

# 450. Building Block Composition Intelligence

At advanced maturity, Architecture Intelligence may dynamically identify suitable Building Blocks for a requirement.

The process may be:

REQUIREMENT
    |
    v
SEMANTIC ANALYSIS
    |
    v
CAPABILITY IDENTIFICATION
    |
    v
BUILDING BLOCK SEARCH
    |
    v
DEPENDENCY ANALYSIS
    |
    v
RISK ANALYSIS
    |
    v
VALUE ANALYSIS
    |
    v
ARCHITECTURE RECOMMENDATION

---

# 451. Building Block Architecture Quality

Building Block quality shall be assessed through:

Reusability

Clarity

Ownership

Interoperability

Security

Reliability

Maintainability

Scalability

Observability

Lifecycle Sustainability

---

The Building Block operating model shall be:

DEFINE

DESIGN

The Building Block Framework shall integrate with:

Domain Model

Transformation

Operations

---

# 454. Integrated Building Block Model

                    MASTER ARCHITECTURE
                            |

                         DOMAINS
                            |

                          LAYERS
                            |

                    BUILDING BLOCKS
                            |
          +-----------------+-----------------+

                            |

---

# 455. Section 4 Summary

EA-IMETA-MASTER-01 Part 2 Section 4 establishes the Architecture Building Block Framework.

The framework introduces reusable architectural components across:

Business

Capabilities

Processes

Information

Data

Applications

Technology

Platforms

AI

Agents

Security

Governance

Risk

Operations

Integration

Architecture Intelligence

Digital Twin

Transformation

Value

The central principle is:

ARCHITECTURE SHOULD NOT BE
REINVENTED WHEN A GOVERNED,
REUSABLE BUILDING BLOCK ALREADY EXISTS.

Building Blocks provide the bridge between:

ARCHITECTURE DESIGN

and

ARCHITECTURE REUSE.

They enable EA-IMETA to progress from documenting architecture toward actively composing architecture.

The long-term target is:

REUSABLE ARCHITECTURE

+

GOVERNED COMPOSITION

+

INTELLIGENT SELECTION

+

CONTINUOUS EVOLUTION

The Architecture Building Block Framework therefore becomes a foundational mechanism for scalable enterprise architecture, architecture standardization, transformation acceleration, and intelligent architecture composition.

---

# 456. EA-IMETA Architecture Reference Model

## 456.1 Purpose

The EA-IMETA Architecture Reference Model establishes the common reference structure against which enterprise architectures, solutions, platforms, transformation initiatives, and architecture decisions may be assessed.

The Reference Model provides a stable architectural baseline while allowing individual enterprises to adapt the model to their specific:

Business Model

Operating Model

Industry

Regulatory Environment

Technology Landscape

Organizational Structure

Strategic Objectives

Transformation Requirements

The purpose is not to prescribe one implementation.

The purpose is to establish a common architectural reference.

---

# 457. Reference Architecture Principle

The EA-IMETA Reference Model shall establish:

COMMON STRUCTURE

COMMON LANGUAGE

COMMON RELATIONSHIPS

COMMON ARCHITECTURAL EXPECTATIONS

while allowing:

LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION

LOCAL ORGANIZATION

LOCAL TECHNOLOGY

LOCAL GOVERNANCE

LOCAL MATURITY

The Reference Model shall therefore be:

STANDARDIZED

but not:

RIGID.

---

# 458. Reference Model Structure

The canonical reference model shall consist of:

PURPOSE
   |
   v
STRATEGY
   |
   v
BUSINESS
   |
   v
CAPABILITY
   |
   v
VALUE
   |
   v
PROCESS
   |
   v
SERVICE
   |
   v

   |
   v
DATA
   |
   v

   |
   v
PLATFORM
   |
   v

   |
   v
AI
   |
   v
AGENTS
   |
   v
OPERATIONS
   |
   v
OBSERVATION
   |
   v
INTELLIGENCE
   |
   v
TRANSFORMATION
   |
   v
EVOLUTION

Cross-cutting:

SECURITY
GOVERNANCE
RISK
COMPLIANCE
RESILIENCE

---

# 459. Reference Model Domains

The Reference Model shall provide reference structures for:

Enterprise Strategy

Business Architecture

Capability Architecture

Value Architecture

Process Architecture

Information Architecture

Data Architecture

Application Architecture

Integration Architecture

Technology Architecture

Cloud Architecture

AI Architecture

Agent Architecture

Security Architecture

Governance Architecture

Risk Architecture

Operations Architecture

Architecture Intelligence

Transformation Architecture

Digital Twin Architecture

---

# 460. Reference Model and Enterprise Context

The Reference Model shall always be interpreted within enterprise context.

The architecture process shall therefore begin with:

ENTERPRISE CONTEXT
       |
       v
STRATEGIC DRIVERS
       |
       v
BUSINESS MODEL
       |
       v
ARCHITECTURE REQUIREMENTS
       |
       v
REFERENCE MODEL
       |
       v
ENTERPRISE-SPECIFIC ARCHITECTURE

The Reference Model shall never replace enterprise analysis.

---

# 461. Reference Model Adaptation

An enterprise may:

Adopt

Adapt

Extend

Combine

Restrict

Replace

Retire

specific reference components.

Any material deviation shall be documented.

---

# 462. Reference Model Conformance

Conformance shall not mean identical implementation.

Conformance shall mean that the enterprise architecture:

Recognizes the required architectural concern

Defines an appropriate architectural representation

Maintains necessary relationships

Provides governance

Maintains traceability

Manages risk

Supports enterprise objectives

---

# 463. Reference Model Conformance Levels

The following conformance levels shall be supported:

LEVEL 0
NOT ADDRESSED

LEVEL 1
RECOGNIZED

LEVEL 2
DEFINED

LEVEL 3
IMPLEMENTED

LEVEL 4
GOVERNED

LEVEL 5
MEASURED

LEVEL 6
INTELLIGENTLY OPTIMIZED

---

# 464. Reference Model Level 0

## 464.1 Not Addressed

The architectural concern is absent or unmanaged.

Example:

No defined data ownership.

---

# 465. Reference Model Level 1

## 465.1 Recognized

The enterprise recognizes the architectural concern but has limited formal definition.

---

# 466. Reference Model Level 2

## 466.1 Defined

The concern has:

Definition

Owner

Scope

Requirements

Relationships

---

# 467. Reference Model Level 3

## 467.1 Implemented

The architecture is represented in actual enterprise implementation.

---

# 468. Reference Model Level 4

## 468.1 Governed

The architecture is subject to:

Policies

Standards

Reviews

Controls

Decision Rights

---

# 469. Reference Model Level 5

## 469.1 Measured

The enterprise measures:

Performance

Risk

Value

Quality

Compliance

Architecture Health

---

# 470. Reference Model Level 6

## 470.1 Intelligently Optimized

Architecture Intelligence continuously supports:

Analysis

Prediction

Optimization

Scenario Evaluation

Decision Support

Architecture Evolution

---

# 471. Reference Architecture Principles

Reference architectures shall follow these principles:

Business First

Capability Orientation

Value Orientation

Reuse

Modularity

Interoperability

Security by Design

Governance by Design

Data by Design

AI by Design

Resilience by Design

Observability by Design

Evolution by Design

---

# 472. Business First Principle

Technology shall not become the primary driver of architecture without explicit strategic justification.

The architecture shall begin with:

Purpose

Business Need

Stakeholder Need

Capability

Outcome

Value

---

# 473. Capability Orientation

Architecture shall organize major enterprise structures around capabilities.

A capability shall provide a stable abstraction between:

Strategy

Business

Implementation

---

# 474. Value Orientation

Architecture decisions shall ultimately be connected to:

Outcomes

Benefits

Value

Cost

Risk

Strategic Objectives

---

# 475. Modularity Principle

Architecture should be composed of modular components where practical.

Modularity should support:

Change

Reuse

Scaling

Replacement

Testing

Governance

---

# 476. Interoperability Principle

Architecture components shall be capable of interoperating through defined interfaces and standards.

Interoperability may include:

Application

Data

Service

Technology

AI

Agent

Human

---

# 477. Security by Design

Security shall be embedded from the beginning.

Security shall not be added only after implementation.

The architecture shall consider:

Identity

Trust

Authorization

Data Protection

Threats

Vulnerabilities

Monitoring

Response

---

# 478. Governance by Design

Governance shall be embedded in architecture structures.

Each material architectural component shall have:

Owner

Authority

Lifecycle

Decision Rights

Controls

Review

---

# 479. Data by Design

Data requirements shall be established during architecture design.

The architecture shall identify:

Data Ownership

Data Quality

Data Classification

Data Lifecycle

Data Access

Data Protection

Data Lineage

---

# 480. AI by Design

Where AI is relevant, architecture shall consider AI requirements from the beginning.

These may include:

Model Selection

Data Requirements

Evaluation

Explainability

Security

Human Oversight

Monitoring

Risk

Lifecycle

---

# 481. Resilience by Design

Architecture shall consider failure as a normal possibility.

Design shall therefore address:

Redundancy

Recovery

Continuity

Failover

Degradation

Incident Response

---

# 482. Observability by Design

Systems and services shall provide sufficient evidence to determine:

What is happening

Why it is happening

What changed

Who or what caused it

What impact occurred

---

# 531. Dynamic Reference Architecture

At advanced maturity, the Reference Architecture shall be continuously improved using:

Operational Evidence

Architecture Analytics

Technology Evolution

Business Change

Risk Changes

Regulatory Changes

AI Insights

Transformation Results

---

# 532. Reference Architecture Feedback Loop

REFERENCE ARCHITECTURE
        |
        v
IMPLEMENTATION
        |
        v
OPERATIONS
        |
        v
OBSERVATION
        |
        v
EVIDENCE
        |
        v
ARCHITECTURE INTELLIGENCE
        |
        v
REFERENCE MODEL IMPROVEMENT
        |
        v
UPDATED REFERENCE ARCHITECTURE

---

# 533. Reference Architecture as a Living System

The Reference Architecture shall not be treated as a static document.

It shall evolve as:

Technology evolves

Business evolves

Regulation evolves

Security evolves

AI evolves

Operating models evolve

Enterprise capabilities evolve

---

# 534. Reference Architecture Lifecycle

The lifecycle shall be:

DISCOVER

↓

DEFINE

↓

DESIGN

↓

REVIEW

↓

APPROVE

↓

PUBLISH

↓

ADOPT

↓

MEASURE

↓

LEARN

↓

EVOLVE

---

# 535. Reference Architecture Quality

Quality shall be assessed according to:

Clarity

Completeness

Consistency

Reusability

Traceability

Governance

Interoperability

Security

Resilience

Operational Feasibility

Value Contribution

---

# 536. Reference Architecture Anti-Patterns

The following shall be avoided:

Reference Architecture Without Business Purpose

Technology-Only Reference Architecture

Overly Prescriptive Architecture

Outdated Reference Architecture

Unmanaged Exceptions

No Ownership

No Lifecycle

No Operational Feedback

No Traceability

No Governance

---

# 537. Reference Architecture and Enterprise Architecture

The relationship shall be:

EA-IMETA MASTER ARCHITECTURE
          |
          v
REFERENCE MODEL
          |
          v
REFERENCE ARCHITECTURES
          |
          v
ENTERPRISE ARCHITECTURE
          |
          v
SOLUTION ARCHITECTURE
          |
          v
IMPLEMENTATION

This establishes a controlled progression from common architecture principles to specific implementation.

---

# 538. Reference Architecture and Solution Architecture

Solution Architecture shall consume relevant Reference Architecture components.

A Solution Architecture shall identify:

Applicable Reference Architecture

Selected Building Blocks

Architecture Decisions

Exceptions

Dependencies

Risks

Implementation

---

# 539. Solution Conformance

A solution shall be evaluated against:

Reference Architecture

Enterprise Standards

Security

Data

Technology

AI

Operations

Governance

---

# 540. Reference Architecture and Portfolio

The enterprise portfolio shall identify which initiatives:

Reuse Reference Architectures

Create New Reference Components

Require Exceptions

Retire Existing Components

---

# 541. Reference Architecture and Investment

Architecture investments shall consider:

Reuse

Standardization

Long-Term Value

Lifecycle Cost

Risk Reduction

Strategic Alignment

Transformation Enablement

---

# 542. Reference Architecture and Technical Debt

Reference Architecture shall actively reduce technical debt by providing:

Approved Patterns

Approved Building Blocks

Approved Platforms

Standard Interfaces

Lifecycle Rules

Modernization Paths

---

# 543. Reference Architecture and Innovation

Innovation shall not be excluded by reference architecture.

Instead, innovation shall be governed through:

Controlled Experimentation

Architecture Sandbox

Pilot Architecture

Innovation Review

Transition Criteria

Reference Model Adoption

---

# 544. Reference Architecture and Architecture Sandbox

An Architecture Sandbox may allow experimental:

Technology

AI

Agent

Integration

Data

Automation

Architecture Patterns

Experiments shall have:

Scope

Owner

Risk Boundary

Time Limit

Evaluation Criteria

Exit Criteria

---

# 545. Reference Architecture and Emerging Technology

Emerging technology shall be evaluated according to:

Strategic Relevance

Capability Contribution

Maturity

Risk

Security

Cost

Interoperability

Vendor Dependency

Architecture Fit

---

# 546. Reference Architecture and AI Evolution

AI-specific Reference Architecture shall evolve as:

Models improve

Platforms change

Regulations change

Risks evolve

Agent capabilities increase

Enterprise use cases expand

---

# 547. Reference Architecture and Agent Evolution

Agent architecture shall evolve from:

ASSISTIVE

to:

SUPERVISED

to:

SEMI-AUTONOMOUS

to:

CONTROLLED AUTONOMOUS

to:

MULTI-AGENT

to:

ENTERPRISE AUTONOMOUS

Each maturity increase shall require corresponding governance maturity.

---

# 548. Reference Architecture and Autonomous Enterprise

The long-term EA-IMETA target may support an autonomous enterprise architecture in which:

Data provides context

AI provides intelligence

Agents provide action

Platforms provide execution

Governance provides boundaries

Humans provide strategic authority

Architecture Intelligence provides continuous optimization

---

# 549. Autonomous Reference Architecture

STRATEGY
   |
   v
INTENT
   |
   v
AI / INTELLIGENCE
   |
   v
AGENTS
   |
   v
AUTHORIZED ACTION
   |
   v
OPERATIONS
   |
   v
OBSERVATION
   |
   v
LEARNING
   |
   v
ARCHITECTURE INTELLIGENCE
   |
   v
STRATEGIC ADAPTATION

Human governance remains authoritative.

---

# 550. Human Authority Principle

No autonomous architecture capability shall eliminate defined human accountability.

The architecture shall distinguish between:

AI Recommendation

Agent Action

Human Approval

Human Decision

Automated Execution

The applicable authority model shall depend on risk and context.

---

# 551. Reference Architecture and Human-in-the-Loop

Human involvement may occur at:

Approval

Exception

Escalation

High-Risk Decision

Strategic Decision

Regulatory Decision

Irreversible Action

---

# 552. Reference Architecture and Human-on-the-Loop

For lower-risk autonomous processes, humans may supervise rather than approve each action.

The architecture shall provide:

Monitoring

Alerts

Audit

Intervention

Emergency Stop

---

# 553. Reference Architecture and Human-out-of-the-Loop

Fully autonomous execution may only be appropriate where:

Risk is sufficiently low

Authority is explicit

Controls are effective

Actions are reversible or bounded

Monitoring is available

Accountability is defined

---

# 554. Reference Architecture Decision Hierarchy

The architecture shall therefore support:

HUMAN DECISION
      |
      v
AI RECOMMENDATION
      |
      v
AGENT EXECUTION
      |
      v
AUTOMATED CONTROL

or, where appropriate:

HUMAN AUTHORIZATION
      |
      v
AGENT EXECUTION
      |
      v
AUTOMATED MONITORING
      |
      v
HUMAN ESCALATION

---

# 555. Reference Architecture Summary

The EA-IMETA Architecture Reference Model provides a common foundation for designing enterprise-specific architectures.

It establishes:

Common Architectural Structure

Common Principles

Reference Domains

Reference Layers

Reference Patterns

Reference Building Blocks

Reference Governance

Reference Conformance

Reference Lifecycle

Reference Intelligence

The model enables:

CONSISTENCY

WITHOUT

UNNECESSARY STANDARDIZATION.

---

# 556. Section 5 Summary

EA-IMETA-MASTER-01 Part 2 Section 5 establishes the Architecture Reference Model.

The Reference Model provides the bridge between:

MASTER ARCHITECTURE

and

ENTERPRISE-SPECIFIC ARCHITECTURE.

It establishes a reusable framework for:

Business

Capability

Value

Process

Information

Data

Application

Integration

Technology

Cloud

AI

Agents

Security

Governance

Risk

Operations

Architecture Intelligence

Digital Twin

Transformation

The Reference Model is designed to remain:

ADAPTABLE

GOVERNED

TRACEABLE

REUSABLE

MEASURABLE

INTELLIGENT

EVOLVABLE

The central principle is:

A REFERENCE ARCHITECTURE SHOULD PROVIDE
A COMMON DIRECTION WITHOUT BECOMING
AN UNNECESSARY CONSTRAINT ON ENTERPRISE
INNOVATION.

The resulting architecture progression is:

MASTER ARCHITECTURE

↓

REFERENCE MODEL

↓

REFERENCE ARCHITECTURE

↓

ENTERPRISE ARCHITECTURE

↓

SOLUTION ARCHITECTURE

↓

IMPLEMENTATION

↓

OPERATIONS

↓

OBSERVATION

↓

ARCHITECTURE INTELLIGENCE

↓

EVOLUTION

This establishes the architectural bridge between strategic enterprise architecture and practical implementation.

# 557. EA-IMETA Architecture Governance and Decision Framework

## 557.1 Purpose

The EA-IMETA Architecture Governance and Decision Framework establishes the mechanisms through which architecture is directed, controlled, reviewed, approved, challenged, measured, and continuously improved.

The framework ensures that architecture decisions are:

Strategically aligned

Business relevant

Traceable

Risk aware

Secure

Governed

Evidence based

Operationally feasible

Value oriented

The objective is to ensure that EA-IMETA is not merely a description of architecture but an active management system for architecture.

---

# 558. Architecture Governance Principle

Architecture governance shall ensure that:

THE RIGHT DECISION

IS MADE

BY THE RIGHT AUTHORITY

AT THE RIGHT TIME

USING THE RIGHT EVIDENCE

WITH CLEAR ACCOUNTABILITY.

Governance shall enable decision making rather than unnecessarily obstruct it.

---

# 559. Governance Objectives

The EA-IMETA governance framework shall provide:

Strategic Alignment

Architecture Consistency

Decision Accountability

Risk Management

Security Assurance

Regulatory Alignment

Investment Alignment

Architecture Quality

Lifecycle Control

Exception Management

Traceability

Continuous Improvement

---

# 560. Governance Scope

Architecture governance shall apply across:

Strategy

Business

Capabilities

Value

Processes

Information

Data

Applications

Integration

Technology

Cloud

AI

Agents

Security

Operations

Architecture Intelligence

Digital Twin

Transformation

---

# 561. Governance Model

The canonical governance model shall be:

```text
ENTERPRISE PURPOSE
        |
        v
STRATEGY
        |
        v
ARCHITECTURE PRINCIPLES
        |
        v
ARCHITECTURE POLICIES
        |
        v
STANDARDS
        |
        v
ARCHITECTURE DECISIONS
        |
        v
IMPLEMENTATION
        |
        v
OPERATIONS
        |
        v
EVIDENCE
        |
        v
GOVERNANCE REVIEW
        |
        v
EVOLUTION
```

---

# 562. Governance Dimensions

Governance shall operate across six primary dimensions:

1. Direction

2. Decision

3. Control

4. Assurance

5. Performance

6. Evolution

---

# 563. Direction Governance

Direction governance establishes:

Strategic Priorities

Architecture Vision

Architecture Principles

Target Architecture

Investment Direction

Transformation Direction

---

# 564. Decision Governance

Decision governance establishes:

Who decides

What they decide

When they decide

What evidence is required

Who must be consulted

Who must be informed

What authority applies

---

# 565. Control Governance

Control governance establishes:

Policies

Standards

Controls

Architecture Constraints

Security Requirements

Risk Boundaries

Compliance Requirements

---

# 566. Assurance Governance

Assurance governance establishes mechanisms to determine whether architecture is:

Compliant

Secure

Effective

Resilient

Operationally Ready

Strategically Aligned

---

# 567. Performance Governance

Performance governance evaluates:

Architecture Health

Business Value

Cost

Risk

Technical Debt

Operational Performance

Transformation Performance

---

# 568. Evolution Governance

Evolution governance ensures that architecture changes in response to:

Business Change

Technology Change

Regulatory Change

Security Change

Market Change

Operational Evidence

Emerging Opportunities

---

# 569. Architecture Authority

The EA-IMETA architecture shall establish an Architecture Authority.

The Architecture Authority is responsible for ensuring that material architecture decisions are made consistently with:

Enterprise Strategy

Architecture Principles

Reference Architecture

Security

Risk

Governance

Investment

Transformation

---

# 570. Architecture Authority Responsibilities

The Architecture Authority shall:

Approve material architecture decisions

Maintain architecture principles

Approve reference architectures

Manage architecture exceptions

Review architecture risks

Monitor architecture health

Resolve architecture conflicts

Maintain architecture standards

Ensure cross-domain alignment

---

# 571. Architecture Governance Bodies

The governance model may include:

Executive Architecture Authority

Enterprise Architecture Board

Domain Architecture Boards

Architecture Review Board

Security Architecture Authority

Data Architecture Authority

AI Governance Authority

Technology Architecture Authority

Transformation Governance

Operational Governance

---

# 572. Governance Hierarchy

The governance hierarchy shall be:

```text
EXECUTIVE GOVERNANCE
        |
        v
ENTERPRISE ARCHITECTURE GOVERNANCE
        |
        v
DOMAIN ARCHITECTURE GOVERNANCE
        |
        v
SOLUTION ARCHITECTURE GOVERNANCE
        |
        v
IMPLEMENTATION GOVERNANCE
        |
        v
OPERATIONAL GOVERNANCE
```

The exact organizational structure may vary by enterprise.

---

# 573. Decision Rights

Decision rights shall be explicitly defined.

Each architecture decision shall identify:

Decision Owner

Decision Authority

Contributors

Approvers

Affected Stakeholders

Implementation Owner

---

# 574. Decision Classes

Architecture decisions shall be classified.

## Class A – Strategic

Enterprise-wide impact.

Examples:

Enterprise Platform Strategy

Major Cloud Strategy

Enterprise AI Strategy

Major Operating Model Change

---

## Class B – Enterprise

Cross-domain or significant enterprise impact.

Examples:

Enterprise Data Platform

Identity Architecture

Integration Platform

---

## Class C – Domain

Primarily affecting one architectural domain.

Examples:

Domain Application Architecture

Domain Data Architecture

Domain Process Architecture

---

## Class D – Solution

Limited solution-specific impact.

Examples:

Application Component

Integration Design

Local Technical Choice

---

## Class E – Operational

Routine operational architecture decisions.

Examples:

Configuration

Minor Version Change

Operational Optimization

---

# 575. Decision Authority Matrix

A decision authority matrix shall define:

| Decision Class | Primary Authority | Required Review |
|---|---|---|
| Strategic | Executive Architecture Authority | Enterprise Architecture |
| Enterprise | Architecture Board | Relevant Domains |
| Domain | Domain Architecture Authority | Enterprise where required |
| Solution | Solution Architecture Authority | Domain Architecture |
| Operational | Operational Authority | Architecture where material |

The matrix shall be adapted to the enterprise governance structure.

---

# 576. Architecture Principles

Architecture principles provide the highest-level architectural guidance.

Principles shall be:

Clear

Stable

Actionable

Relevant

Governed

Reviewable

---

# 577. Mandatory Architecture Principles

EA-IMETA shall establish at least the following principles:

Business Value First

Capability Orientation

Reuse Before Reinvention

Security by Design

Data by Design

AI by Design

Governance by Design

Open Integration

Modularity

Resilience

Observability

Traceability

Lifecycle Management

Human Accountability

Continuous Evolution

---

# 578. Principle Hierarchy

The principle hierarchy shall be:

```text
ENTERPRISE PRINCIPLES
        |
        v
ARCHITECTURE PRINCIPLES
        |
        v
DOMAIN PRINCIPLES
        |
        v
TECHNICAL PRINCIPLES
        |
        v
IMPLEMENTATION STANDARDS
```

Lower-level principles shall not contradict higher-level principles without an approved exception.

---

# 579. Architecture Policy

Policies translate principles into mandatory governance requirements.

A policy shall define:

Purpose

Scope

Requirement

Authority

Compliance

Exceptions

Review

---

# 580. Architecture Standards

Standards translate policy into repeatable technical or architectural expectations.

Examples:

API Standards

Data Standards

Identity Standards

Cloud Standards

AI Standards

Agent Standards

Logging Standards

Security Standards

Integration Standards

---

# 581. Governance Artifact Hierarchy

The governance artifact hierarchy shall be:

```text
PURPOSE
   |
   v
PRINCIPLE
   |
   v
POLICY
   |
   v
STANDARD
   |
   v
PATTERN
   |
   v
BUILDING BLOCK
   |
   v
IMPLEMENTATION
   |
   v
EVIDENCE
```

---

# 582. Architecture Decision Record

Material architecture decisions shall be recorded using an Architecture Decision Record.

An ADR shall contain:

Decision ID

Title

Context

Problem

Options

Evaluation

Decision

Rationale

Consequences

Risks

Dependencies

Owner

Approval

Date

Review Date

---

# 583. Architecture Decision Lifecycle

```text
IDENTIFY
   |
   v
ANALYSE
   |
   v
OPTIONS
   |
   v
EVALUATE
   |
   v
DECIDE
   |
   v
APPROVE
   |
   v
IMPLEMENT
   |
   v
MONITOR
   |
   v
REVIEW
   |
   v
RETIRE / REPLACE
```

---

# 584. Decision Evidence

Architecture decisions should be supported by evidence.

Evidence may include:

Business Requirements

Cost Analysis

Risk Analysis

Security Assessment

Performance Data

Architecture Models

Operational Data

Compliance Requirements

Vendor Information

Architecture Intelligence

---

# 585. Decision Quality

Decision quality shall be evaluated according to:

Strategic Alignment

Evidence Quality

Alternative Analysis

Risk Awareness

Value

Reversibility

Lifecycle Impact

Operational Impact

---

# 586. Reversible and Irreversible Decisions

Architecture decisions shall distinguish between:

Reversible Decisions

and

Irreversible Decisions.

Reversible decisions may be made faster with lighter governance.

Irreversible or high-cost decisions require stronger governance.

---

# 587. Architecture Review

Architecture Review shall assess:

Alignment

Completeness

Consistency

Security

Risk

Data

Technology

AI

Operations

Value

---

# 588. Architecture Review Gates

Architecture may use formal review gates.

```text
GATE 0
IDEA

   |

GATE 1
ARCHITECTURE CONCEPT

   |

GATE 2
ARCHITECTURE DESIGN

   |

GATE 3
APPROVAL

   |

GATE 4
IMPLEMENTATION READINESS

   |

GATE 5
OPERATIONAL READINESS

   |

GATE 6
POST-IMPLEMENTATION REVIEW
```

---

# 589. Gate 0 – Idea

The idea shall establish:

Problem

Opportunity

Initial Objective

Potential Value

Initial Scope

---

# 590. Gate 1 – Architecture Concept

The architecture concept shall establish:

Context

Capabilities

Stakeholders

Initial Architecture

Dependencies

Risks

---

# 591. Gate 2 – Architecture Design

The detailed design shall establish:

Architecture Layers

Building Blocks

Interfaces

Data

Security

AI

Operations

Dependencies

---

# 592. Gate 3 – Approval

Approval shall confirm:

Architecture Alignment

Risk Acceptance

Security

Governance

Investment

---

# 593. Gate 4 – Implementation Readiness

The solution shall demonstrate:

Design Completeness

Dependencies

Security

Operational Design

Data Readiness

Implementation Plan

---

# 594. Gate 5 – Operational Readiness

The solution shall demonstrate:

Monitoring

Support

Security

Recovery

Performance

Documentation

Ownership

---

# 595. Gate 6 – Post-Implementation Review

The review shall evaluate:

Expected Value

Actual Value

Architecture Conformance

Operational Performance

Risk

Lessons Learned

Architecture Debt

---

# 596. Architecture Exception Management

An exception shall be used when an architecture implementation cannot meet an approved requirement.

Exceptions shall not become a permanent substitute for architecture governance.

---

# 597. Exception Request

An exception request shall contain:

Exception ID

Requirement

Affected Architecture

Reason

Alternative

Risk

Impact

Mitigation

Duration

Owner

Approval

Review Date

---

# 598. Exception Lifecycle

```text
REQUEST
   |
   v
ASSESS
   |
   v
RISK REVIEW
   |
   v
DECISION
   |
   +---- REJECT
   |
   v
APPROVE
   |
   v
MONITOR
   |
   v
REVIEW
   |
   v
CLOSE / EXTEND
```

---

# 599. Exception Principle

Exceptions shall be:

Specific

Time-Bounded

Owned

Risk-Assessed

Reviewable

Traceable

---

# 600. Architecture Compliance

Architecture compliance shall assess whether implementation follows:

Principles

Policies

Standards

Reference Architectures

Approved Decisions

Security Requirements

Data Requirements

---

# 601. Compliance Levels

Compliance may be:

Compliant

Conditionally Compliant

Partially Compliant

Non-Compliant

Exception Approved

Not Assessed

---

# 602. Continuous Architecture Compliance

Architecture compliance should move from periodic manual review toward continuous assessment.

The target model is:

```text
ARCHITECTURE
      |
      v
IMPLEMENTATION
      |
      v
OBSERVATION
      |
      v
AUTOMATED CONFORMANCE
      |
      v
DEVIATION
      |
      v
RISK ASSESSMENT
      |
      v
REMEDIATION
```

---

# 603. Architecture Drift Governance

Architecture drift shall be governed when operational reality diverges from approved architecture.

Drift may result from:

Unauthorized Change

Technology Evolution

Configuration Change

Emergency Change

Shadow IT

Unmanaged Integration

Vendor Change

Data Change

AI Model Change

Agent Behavior

---

# 604. Drift Classification

Drift shall be classified as:

Informational

Low

Moderate

High

Critical

Classification shall consider:

Impact

Risk

Scope

Duration

Reversibility

---

# 605. Architecture Risk

Architecture governance shall maintain an architecture risk register.

Risk categories may include:

Strategic

Business

Technology

Data

Security

AI

Agent

Operational

Financial

Compliance

Vendor

Resilience

---

# 606. Architecture Risk Register

Each risk should contain:

Risk ID

Description

Cause

Impact

Likelihood

Risk Owner

Mitigation

Residual Risk

Status

Review Date

---

# 607. Architecture Risk Escalation

Risks shall escalate according to:

Impact

Likelihood

Urgency

Regulatory Exposure

Business Criticality

Security Exposure

---

# 608. Architecture Security Governance

Security shall be integrated into architecture governance.

Security review shall consider:

Identity

Access

Trust

Data Protection

Encryption

Threats

Vulnerabilities

Monitoring

Incident Response

---

# 609. AI Governance

AI architecture shall require additional governance for:

Model Risk

Data Risk

Bias

Performance

Security

Transparency

Human Oversight

Monitoring

Model Drift

Lifecycle

---

# 610. Agent Governance

Agent governance shall control:

Agent Identity

Authority

Permissions

Tools

Data Access

Actions

Decision Scope

Escalation

Audit

Termination

---

# 611. Agent Authority Matrix

Each agent shall have an authority profile:

```text
AGENT
  |
  +-- Identity
  +-- Role
  +-- Purpose
  +-- Permissions
  +-- Tools
  +-- Data
  +-- Actions
  +-- Limits
  +-- Escalation
```

---

# 612. Human Accountability

Human accountability shall remain explicit.

For material decisions, the architecture shall identify:

Human Owner

AI Role

Agent Role

Approval Authority

Escalation Authority

---

# 613. Governance of Autonomous Actions

Autonomous actions shall be classified according to risk.

Low Risk:

May be automated.

Moderate Risk:

May require monitoring or defined approval.

High Risk:

Requires human authorization unless explicitly governed otherwise.

Critical or irreversible:

Requires explicit human control.

---

# 614. Architecture Investment Governance

Architecture governance shall be connected to investment decisions.

Investment proposals shall identify:

Strategic Alignment

Capability Impact

Architecture Impact

Cost

Risk

Benefits

Lifecycle Cost

Dependencies

---

# 615. Architecture Portfolio Governance

The architecture portfolio shall provide visibility of:

Applications

Platforms

Technology

Data Products

AI Models

Agents

Transformation Initiatives

Technical Debt

Architecture Risks

---

# 616. Architecture Debt Governance

Architecture debt shall be actively managed.

Debt may include:

Outdated Technology

Duplicate Systems

Unsupported Components

Unmanaged Interfaces

Data Quality Problems

Security Weaknesses

Architecture Exceptions

---

# 617. Architecture Debt Register

The register should contain:

Debt ID

Description

Cause

Impact

Risk

Cost

Owner

Priority

Remediation

Target Date

---

# 618. Architecture Debt Prioritization

Debt should be prioritized using:

Business Impact

Risk

Cost

Urgency

Dependency

Strategic Relevance

---

# 619. Architecture Governance Metrics

The governance framework shall measure:

Decision Cycle Time

Exception Rate

Compliance Rate

Architecture Drift

Architecture Debt

Risk Exposure

Review Completion

Reference Reuse

Standards Adoption

Value Realization

---

# 620. Governance Performance Dashboard

A governance dashboard may provide:

```text
ARCHITECTURE HEALTH
        |
        +-- Compliance
        +-- Risk
        +-- Debt
        +-- Exceptions
        +-- Drift
        +-- Decisions
        +-- Value
        +-- Transformation
```

---

# 621. Governance Transparency

Governance decisions should be transparent to authorized stakeholders.

The architecture repository should provide:

Decision History

Approval History

Exception History

Risk History

Architecture Changes

Standards

Reference Architectures

---

# 622. Governance Traceability

Every material decision shall be traceable to:

Requirement

Purpose

Strategy

Capability

Architecture

Risk

Evidence

Decision

Implementation

Outcome

---

# 623. Governance Knowledge Graph

Governance relationships shall be represented in the EA-IMETA Knowledge Graph.

Example:

```text
[STRATEGY]
    |
    v
[REQUIREMENT]
    |
    v
[ARCHITECTURE DECISION]
    |
    +---- [RISK]
    |
    +---- [STANDARD]
    |
    +---- [BUILDING BLOCK]
    |
    v
[IMPLEMENTATION]
    |
    v
[OUTCOME]
```

---

# 624. Architecture Intelligence for Governance

Architecture Intelligence may support governance through:

Decision Analysis

Risk Detection

Compliance Analysis

Dependency Analysis

Impact Analysis

Exception Detection

Architecture Drift Detection

Recommendation

---

# 625. AI-Assisted Governance

AI may assist governance activities by:

Summarizing architecture

Identifying dependencies

Comparing alternatives

Detecting inconsistencies

Finding relevant standards

Identifying risks

Preparing review material

Suggesting questions

However:

AI SHALL SUPPORT GOVERNANCE.

AI SHALL NOT AUTOMATICALLY BECOME THE GOVERNANCE AUTHORITY.

---

# 626. Governance Automation

Governance activities may be automated where risk permits.

Examples:

Policy Checks

Configuration Checks

Security Checks

Architecture Conformance

Naming Standards

Interface Standards

Lifecycle Checks

---

# 627. Governance Automation Boundary

Automation shall remain subject to:

Defined Authority

Auditability

Exception Handling

Human Escalation

Override

Emergency Control

---

# 628. Architecture Governance Operating Cycle

The operating cycle shall be:

```text

  |
  v
DESIGN
  |
  v
REVIEW
  |
  v
DECIDE
  |
  v
IMPLEMENT
  |
  v
MONITOR
  |
  v
ASSURE
  |
  v
LEARN
  |
  v
```
