
---

# 629. Governance and Continuous Improvement

Governance shall not only identify non-compliance.

It shall identify opportunities to improve:

Architecture

Standards

Processes

Building Blocks

Reference Architectures

Decision Quality

Governance Efficiency

---

# 630. Governance Maturity

EA-IMETA governance maturity shall progress through:

LEVEL 1
AD HOC GOVERNANCE

LEVEL 2
DEFINED GOVERNANCE

LEVEL 3
STANDARDIZED GOVERNANCE

LEVEL 4
MEASURED GOVERNANCE

LEVEL 5
INTELLIGENT GOVERNANCE

LEVEL 6
ADAPTIVE GOVERNANCE

---

# 631. Level 1 – Ad Hoc Governance

Decisions are made individually without consistent governance.

---

# 632. Level 2 – Defined Governance

Roles, responsibilities, and decision processes are defined.

---

# 633. Level 3 – Standardized Governance

Policies, standards, review gates, and decision records are consistently applied.

---

# 634. Level 4 – Measured Governance

Governance performance and architecture outcomes are measured.

---

# 635. Level 5 – Intelligent Governance

Architecture Intelligence supports:

Risk Detection

Impact Analysis

Compliance

Decision Support

---

# 636. Level 6 – Adaptive Governance

Governance dynamically adapts to:

Risk

Business Change

Technology Change

Regulatory Change

Operational Evidence

Architecture Intelligence

---

# 637. Governance and Transformation

Transformation programs shall operate under architecture governance.

Each transformation initiative should identify:

Strategic Objective

Capability Impact

Current State

Target State

Architecture Decisions

Dependencies

Risks

Benefits

---

# 638. Transformation Decision Governance

Transformation decisions shall consider:

Architecture Alignment

Business Value

Investment

Risk

Dependencies

Change Capacity

Operational Impact

---

# 639. Governance and Digital Twin

Digital Twin capabilities may provide governance evidence through:

Current State

Operational State

Dependency State

Risk State

Simulation

Scenario Analysis

---

# 640. Governance and Enterprise Autonomy

In an autonomous enterprise, governance becomes more important rather than less important.

Autonomy requires explicit:

Identity

Authority

Policy

Boundaries

Controls

Monitoring

Escalation

Accountability

---

# 641. Autonomous Governance Model

```text
POLICY
   |
   v
AUTHORITY
   |
   v
AGENT
   |
   v
ACTION
   |
   v
MONITORING
   |
   v
CONTROL
   |
   v
HUMAN OVERSIGHT
```

---

# 642. Architecture Governance as a Control System

EA-IMETA shall treat governance as a control system.

The model is:

```text
TARGET
  |
  v
ARCHITECTURE
  |
  v
IMPLEMENTATION
  |
  v
OBSERVATION
  |
  v
COMPARISON
  |
  v
DEVIATION
  |
  v
CORRECTION
  |
  v
NEW STATE
```

This creates a continuous architecture control loop.

---

# 643. Governance Feedback

Feedback may originate from:

Business

Operations

Security

Data

AI

Agents

Users

Regulators

Technology

Transformation

Architecture Intelligence

---

# 644. Governance Feedback to Strategy

Where architecture evidence indicates that strategic assumptions are no longer valid, governance shall be capable of escalating findings back to strategy.

The relationship is therefore:

```text
OPERATIONS
   |
   v
ARCHITECTURE INTELLIGENCE
   |
   v
ARCHITECTURE GOVERNANCE
   |
   v
STRATEGY REVIEW
   |
   v
UPDATED DIRECTION
```

---

# 645. Governance as Enterprise Learning

Governance shall create institutional learning by retaining:

Decisions

Rationale

Outcomes

Failures

Exceptions

Risks

Lessons

Patterns

Standards

---

# 646. Architecture Decision Memory

The architecture repository shall preserve decision history.

This prevents repeated debate over previously resolved architectural questions and enables future architects to understand:

What was decided

Why it was decided

What evidence existed

What assumptions were made

What happened afterwards

---

# 647. Governance and Knowledge Management

Governance knowledge shall be connected to:

Architecture Repository

Knowledge Graph

Documents

Standards

Policies

Decisions

Operational Evidence

Lessons Learned

---

# 648. Governance Quality Principles

Governance shall be:

Proportionate

Transparent

Accountable

Evidence-Based

Risk-Based

Timely

Traceable

Adaptive

---

# 649. Proportional Governance

Governance effort shall be proportional to:

Risk

Impact

Cost

Irreversibility

Scope

Strategic Importance

---

# 650. Governance Anti-Patterns

The following shall be avoided:

Governance by Bureaucracy

Unclear Authority

Too Many Approval Layers

No Decision Owner

Decisions Without Evidence

Unmanaged Exceptions

Architecture Without Enforcement

Enforcement Without Context

AI Governance Without Human Accountability

---

# 651. Governance and Architecture Repository

The architecture repository shall be the authoritative source for:

Architecture Decisions

Principles

Policies

Standards

Exceptions

Architecture Reviews

Reference Architectures

Building Blocks

Risks

Compliance

---

# 652. Governance Record Retention

Governance records shall be retained according to:

Legal Requirements

Regulatory Requirements

Enterprise Policy

Architecture Lifecycle Requirements

Historical Traceability

---

# 653. Governance and Change Management

Architecture governance shall integrate with enterprise change management.

Material changes shall identify:

Architecture Impact

Security Impact

Data Impact

Operational Impact

Business Impact

Risk

Approval

---

# 654. Emergency Architecture Change

Emergency changes may bypass normal review only where necessary to:

Protect People

Protect Critical Services

Protect Data

Respond to Security Incidents

Restore Operations

Emergency decisions shall receive retrospective architecture review.

---

# 655. Post-Emergency Review

The review shall establish:

What Happened

Why It Happened

What Was Changed

What Risk Was Accepted

Whether Architecture Should Change

Whether Standards Should Change

Whether Preventive Controls Are Required

---

# 656. Governance and Lifecycle

Governance shall apply throughout the architecture lifecycle:

Concept

Design

Approval

Implementation

Operation

Modernization

Retirement

---

# 657. Governance Lifecycle Model

```text
CONCEPT
   |
   v
DESIGN
   |
   v
DECISION
   |
   v
IMPLEMENTATION
   |
   v
OPERATION
   |
   v
REVIEW
   |
   v
MODERNIZATION
   |
   v
RETIREMENT
```

---

# 658. Governance and Retirement

Retirement decisions shall consider:

Business Impact

Dependencies

Data Retention

Security

Migration

Cost

Operational Risk

Replacement

Historical Traceability

---

# 659. Governance and Value

Architecture governance shall ultimately ensure that architecture contributes to enterprise value.

The value chain is:

```text
STRATEGY
   |
   v
ARCHITECTURE
   |
   v
INVESTMENT
   |
   v
CAPABILITY
   |
   v
EXECUTION
   |
   v
OUTCOME
   |
   v
VALUE
```

---

# 660. Governance and Architecture Health

Architecture health shall be evaluated through:

Strategic Alignment

Business Alignment

Technology Health

Data Health

Security

Risk

Resilience

Technical Debt

Operational Performance

Value

---

# 661. Governance Scorecard

A governance scorecard may include:

Architecture Compliance

Decision Quality

Exception Rate

Architecture Debt

Risk Exposure

Security Compliance

Standards Adoption

Reference Reuse

Operational Drift

Value Realization

---

# 662. Governance Intelligence

At advanced maturity, governance shall become increasingly predictive.

The system may identify:

Emerging Risk

Likely Architecture Drift

Future Capacity Constraints

Technology Obsolescence

Potential Security Exposure

AI Model Risk

Agent Authority Risk

---

# 663. Predictive Governance

The predictive governance model shall be:

```text
CURRENT STATE
      |
      v
OBSERVATION
      |
      v
TREND ANALYSIS
      |
      v
PREDICTION
      |
      v
RISK
      |
      v
PREVENTIVE ACTION
```

---

# 664. Adaptive Governance

Adaptive governance shall dynamically adjust governance intensity according to:

Risk

Change Velocity

Business Criticality

Technology Volatility

Autonomy Level

Regulatory Exposure

---

# 665. Governance and Autonomous Architecture

As autonomy increases, governance shall evolve from:

MANUAL APPROVAL

to:

POLICY-BASED CONTROL

to:

CONTINUOUS ASSURANCE

to:

INTELLIGENT GOVERNANCE

The objective is not to eliminate human governance but to make governance scalable.

---

# 666. Section 6 Summary

EA-IMETA-MASTER-01 Part 2 Section 6 establishes the Architecture Governance and Decision Framework.

The framework establishes governance for:

Architecture Principles

Policies

Standards

Architecture Decisions

Review Gates

Exceptions

Compliance

Risk

Security

AI

Agents

Investment

Architecture Debt

Transformation

Operations

Architecture Intelligence

The central principle is:

ARCHITECTURE GOVERNANCE SHALL ENABLE
GOOD DECISIONS, NOT SIMPLY CONTROL
ARCHITECTURE.

The governance system therefore establishes a continuous loop:

DIRECTION

↓

DECISION

↓

IMPLEMENTATION

↓

OBSERVATION

↓

ASSURANCE

↓

LEARNING

↓

EVOLUTION

The long-term objective is an adaptive architecture governance capability in which:

Human authority

+

Clear policy

+

Architecture intelligence

+

Continuous evidence

+

Automated assurance

work together to maintain enterprise alignment and control.

The resulting model is:

```text
STRATEGY
   |
   v
ARCHITECTURE
   |
   v
GOVERNANCE
   |
   v
DECISION
   |
   v
IMPLEMENTATION
   |
   v
OPERATIONS
   |
   v
EVIDENCE
   |
   v
INTELLIGENCE
   |
   v
GOVERNANCE
   |
   v
EVOLUTION
```

This establishes governance as a continuous enterprise control and learning mechanism rather than a periodic architecture approval exercise.

---

# 667. EA-IMETA Architecture Intelligence and Knowledge Framework

## 667.1 Purpose

The EA-IMETA Architecture Intelligence and Knowledge Framework establishes the structures through which architecture information is transformed into usable knowledge, analysis, insight, recommendations, decisions, and continuous architectural improvement.

The framework connects:

Architecture Data

Architecture Models

Operational Evidence

Knowledge

Analytics

Artificial Intelligence

Decision Support

Governance

Transformation

The objective is to make architecture:

SEARCHABLE

UNDERSTANDABLE

CONNECTED

ANALYZABLE

PREDICTIVE

ACTIONABLE

EVOLVABLE

---

# 668. Architecture Intelligence Principle

Architecture Intelligence shall not merely store architecture information.

It shall enable the enterprise to understand:

WHAT EXISTS

HOW IT IS CONNECTED

WHY IT EXISTS

WHAT DEPENDS ON IT

WHAT IS CHANGING

WHAT MAY HAPPEN

WHAT SHOULD BE DONE

The fundamental progression is:

DATA

↓

INFORMATION

↓

KNOWLEDGE

↓

INSIGHT

↓

DECISION

↓

ACTION

↓

LEARNING

---

# 669. Architecture Knowledge Model

The architecture knowledge model shall distinguish between:

Architecture Objects

Architecture Relationships

Architecture Events

Architecture Decisions

Architecture Evidence

Architecture Rules

Architecture Patterns

Architecture Standards

Architecture Risks

Architecture Outcomes

Architecture Value

---

# 670. Architecture Intelligence Layers

The Architecture Intelligence capability shall consist of:

1. Architecture Data Layer

2. Semantic Layer

3. Knowledge Graph Layer

4. Analytics Layer

5. Intelligence Layer

6. Decision Support Layer

7. Action and Feedback Layer

---

# 671. Architecture Intelligence Model

```text
ARCHITECTURE DATA
        |
        v
SEMANTIC MODEL
        |
        v
KNOWLEDGE GRAPH
        |
        v
ANALYTICS
        |
        v
AI / INTELLIGENCE
        |
        v
RECOMMENDATION
        |
        v
DECISION
        |
        v
ACTION
        |
        v
OBSERVATION
        |
        v
LEARNING
```

---

# 672. Architecture Data

Architecture Data represents structured information about the enterprise architecture.

Examples include:

Capabilities

Processes

Services

Applications

Data

Technology

Platforms

AI Models

Agents

Controls

Risks

Decisions

Standards

Projects

---

# 673. Architecture Metadata

Every important architecture object should have metadata.

Metadata may include:

Object ID

Name

Type

Description

Owner

Domain

Layer

Status

Lifecycle

Version

Classification

Dependencies

Consumers

Source

Evidence

Last Updated

Confidence

---

# 674. Architecture Data Sources

Architecture Intelligence may consume data from:

Architecture Repository

CMDB

Application Portfolio

Data Catalogue

Identity Systems

Security Systems

Cloud Platforms

Monitoring Systems

Project Systems

Service Management

Financial Systems

Risk Systems

Compliance Systems

AI Platforms

Agent Platforms

---

# 675. Architecture Data Integration

Architecture Intelligence shall integrate data through governed interfaces.

```text
SOURCE SYSTEMS
      |
      +---- Architecture Repository
      +---- CMDB
      +---- Data Catalogue
      +---- Security
      +---- Operations
      +---- Finance
      +---- Projects
      +---- AI Platforms
      |
      v
ARCHITECTURE DATA INTEGRATION
      |
      v
ARCHITECTURE KNOWLEDGE FOUNDATION
```

---

# 676. Data Quality

Architecture Intelligence is dependent on architecture data quality.

Data quality dimensions shall include:

Accuracy

Completeness

Consistency

Timeliness

Uniqueness

Validity

Traceability

---

# 677. Architecture Data Confidence

Architecture Intelligence shall distinguish between:

Confirmed

Verified

Inferred

Estimated

Unknown

Conflicting

The system shall not present inferred information as confirmed fact.

---

# 678. Semantic Architecture Model

The semantic model establishes common meaning for architecture concepts.

Examples:

Capability

Application

Service

Data Product

Technology

Risk

Control

Agent

Decision

Outcome

---

# 679. Semantic Consistency

The same enterprise concept should have a consistent meaning across:

Business

Architecture

Data

Technology

Security

Operations

AI

Governance

---

# 680. Architecture Ontology

The EA-IMETA ontology shall define relationships between architecture concepts.

Examples:

REALIZES

SUPPORTS

DEPENDS_ON

CONSUMES

PROVIDES

HOSTS

PROTECTS

GOVERNS

IMPLEMENTS

REQUIRES

IMPACTS

REPLACES

---

# 681. Architecture Knowledge Graph

The Knowledge Graph shall represent architecture objects as nodes and relationships as edges.

Example:

```text
[CAPABILITY]
      |
      | realized-by
      v
[APPLICATION]
      |
      | runs-on
      v
[PLATFORM]
      |
      | protected-by
      v
[SECURITY CONTROL]
```

---

# 682. Knowledge Graph Scope

The Knowledge Graph shall be capable of connecting:

Strategy

Capabilities

Processes

Services

Information

Data

Applications

Technology

AI

Agents

Security

Governance

Operations

Transformation

Value

---

# 683. Knowledge Graph Traceability

The Knowledge Graph shall support traceability such as:

Strategy → Capability

Capability → Process

Process → Service

Service → Application

Application → Technology

Data → Application

Risk → Control

Decision → Implementation

Implementation → Outcome

Outcome → Value

---

# 684. Architecture Graph Traversal

Architecture Intelligence shall support graph traversal.

Example:

```text
TECHNOLOGY
   |
   v
APPLICATION
   |
   v
SERVICE
   |
   v
PROCESS
   |
   v
CAPABILITY
   |
   v
STRATEGIC OBJECTIVE
```

This allows impact analysis from technical components to strategic objectives.

---

# 685. Reverse Traversal

The graph shall also support reverse analysis.

Example:

```text
STRATEGIC OBJECTIVE
   |
   v
CAPABILITY
   |
   v
APPLICATION
   |
   v
TECHNOLOGY
```

This enables the question:

Which technology supports this strategic objective?

---

# 686. Architecture Search

Architecture Intelligence shall provide semantic architecture search.

Users should be able to search using natural language.

Examples:

"Show applications supporting customer onboarding."

"Which systems depend on platform X?"

"Which capabilities are affected by this database?"

"Which agents can modify customer records?"

---

# 687. Semantic Search

Semantic search shall understand concepts rather than only exact keywords.

For example:

"customer service"

may identify:

Customer Support

Service Desk

Customer Care

Case Management

Support Process

---

# 688. Architecture Discovery

Architecture Intelligence shall support discovery of previously unknown relationships.

Potential discovery sources include:

Application metadata

Network relationships

API traffic

Data lineage

Identity activity

Operational logs

Configuration

Project documentation

---

# 689. Discovered Architecture

Discovered relationships shall be classified as:

Observed

Inferred

Confirmed

Rejected

Pending Validation

---

# 690. Architecture Reconciliation

Discovered architecture shall be reconciled against approved architecture.

```text
APPROVED ARCHITECTURE
        |
        v
DISCOVERED ARCHITECTURE
        |
        v
COMPARE
        |
        v
MATCH / DIFFERENCE
        |
        v
VALIDATE
        |
        v
UPDATE ARCHITECTURE
```

---

Architecture Intelligence shall identify:

Unauthorized Components

Unexpected Dependencies

Unapproved Technology

Unused Systems

Unmanaged Interfaces

Outdated Components

Configuration Deviations

AI Model Changes

Agent Permission Changes

# 692. Dependency Intelligence

Indirect Dependencies

Shared Dependencies

Hidden Dependencies

Circular Dependencies

Single Points of Failure

# 782. Section 7 Summary

EA-IMETA-MASTER-01 Part 2 Section 7 establishes the Architecture Intelligence and Knowledge Framework.

The framework transforms the EA-IMETA architecture from a static repository into an intelligent architectural knowledge system.

It establishes:

Architecture Data

Semantic Architecture

Knowledge Graph

Architecture Search

Architecture Discovery

Dependency Intelligence

Impact Analysis

Scenario Analysis

Predictive Architecture

Risk Intelligence

Recommendation

Architecture Copilot

Architecture Agents

Continuous Compliance

Technical Debt Intelligence

Value Intelligence

AI Architecture Intelligence

Agent Architecture Intelligence

Security Intelligence

Transformation Intelligence

Digital Twin Integration

The central principle is:

ARCHITECTURE INTELLIGENCE SHALL TURN
ARCHITECTURE INFORMATION INTO
ACTIONABLE ENTERPRISE KNOWLEDGE.

The target progression is:

DATA

↓

INFORMATION

↓

KNOWLEDGE

↓

INTELLIGENCE

↓

DECISION

↓

ACTION

↓

OBSERVATION

↓

LEARNING

The long-term EA-IMETA target is therefore an architecture capability that can:

UNDERSTAND THE ENTERPRISE

UNDERSTAND ITS DEPENDENCIES

UNDERSTAND ITS RISKS

UNDERSTAND ITS CHANGES

PREDICT POTENTIAL OUTCOMES

RECOMMEND ARCHITECTURAL ACTIONS

AND CONTINUOUSLY LEARN FROM RESULTS.

Human governance remains authoritative for material decisions.

# 783. EA-IMETA Architecture Transformation and Evolution Framework

## 783.1 Purpose

The EA-IMETA Architecture Transformation and Evolution Framework establishes how the enterprise architecture moves from its current state toward desired future states while remaining capable of continuous adaptation.

The framework connects:

Current State

Strategic Intent

Architecture Vision

Target State

Transition Architectures

Transformation Initiatives

Implementation

Operational Evidence

Architecture Intelligence

Continuous Evolution

The objective is to ensure that architecture transformation is not treated as a collection of isolated projects but as a coordinated enterprise evolution capability.

---

# 784. Transformation Principle

Transformation shall be:

Strategic

Capability Driven

Value Oriented

Architecture Led

Evidence Based

Risk Managed

Incremental Where Appropriate

Governed

Measurable

Evolvable

The fundamental principle is:

TRANSFORM WITH PURPOSE

BUILD WITH CONTROL

MEASURE THE RESULT

LEARN FROM EVIDENCE

EVOLVE CONTINUOUSLY

---

# 785. Architecture Evolution

Architecture evolution describes the continuous change of the enterprise architecture over time.

Evolution may be driven by:

Strategy

Business Change

Market Change

Customer Expectations

Technology

Regulation

Security

Risk

Operational Evidence

AI

Automation

Transformation

---

# 786. Transformation versus Evolution

Transformation represents intentional major change.

Evolution represents continuous architectural adaptation.

The relationship is:

```text
TRANSFORMATION
      |
      v
NEW ARCHITECTURE STATE
      |
      v
CONTINUOUS EVOLUTION
      |
      v
NEXT TRANSFORMATION
```

Transformation and evolution shall therefore be treated as complementary capabilities.

---

# 787. Current State Architecture

The Current State Architecture shall establish a reliable representation of the enterprise as it exists.

It shall include, where relevant:

Business

Capabilities

Processes

Services

Information

Data

Applications

Integration

Technology

Cloud

AI

Agents

Security

Operations

Governance

---

# 788. Current State Quality

Current State Architecture shall distinguish between:

Known

Verified

Observed

Inferred

Estimated

Unknown

This prevents assumptions from being treated as architectural facts.

---

# 789. Architecture Baseline

The approved Current State shall form an architecture baseline.

The baseline shall provide:

Reference Point

Change Detection

Impact Analysis

Transformation Starting Point

Architecture Debt Identification

---

# 790. Target State Architecture

The Target State Architecture shall describe the desired future architecture.

It shall be connected to:

Strategy

Business Outcomes

Capabilities

Value

Transformation Objectives

Risk

Technology Direction

---

# 791. Target State Principle

The Target State shall not describe technology for its own sake.

It shall describe the architectural capabilities required to achieve the desired enterprise outcome.

```text
   v

   v

      |
```

# 886. EA-IMETA Architecture Transformation Principles

The complete transformation framework shall follow these principles:

1. Transform for measurable purpose.

2. Start from business capability.

3. Establish a reliable current state.

4. Define a meaningful target state.

5. Use transition architectures where required.

6. Reuse approved building blocks.

7. Manage dependencies explicitly.

8. Govern architecture decisions.

9. Measure value realization.

10. Learn from operational evidence.

11. Use intelligence to improve transformation.

12. Maintain human accountability.

13. Evolve continuously.

---

Architecture evolution shall follow:

Evidence over assumption

Capability over technology

Value over activity

Reuse over duplication

Security over convenience

Governance over uncontrolled autonomy

Learning over repetition

Adaptation over stagnation

# 901. EA-IMETA Enterprise Architecture Operating Model

## 901.1 Purpose

Part 3 begins the operationalization of the EA-IMETA Master Architecture.

Where Part 1 established the strategic and conceptual foundation, and Part 2 established the structural, governance, intelligence, and transformation mechanisms, Part 3 defines how the architecture operates as an enterprise capability.

The EA-IMETA Enterprise Architecture Operating Model establishes:

Architecture Functions

Architecture Roles

Architecture Services

Architecture Processes

Architecture Responsibilities

Architecture Information Flows

Architecture Decision Flows

Architecture Planning

Architecture Delivery

Architecture Assurance

Architecture Measurement

Architecture Improvement

The objective is to transform EA-IMETA from an architecture framework into a repeatable enterprise operating capability.

---

# 902. Operating Model Principle

Enterprise Architecture shall operate as a continuous enterprise capability rather than as a periodic documentation activity.

The fundamental operating model is:

```text
STRATEGY
   |
   v
ARCHITECTURE
   |
   v
GOVERNANCE
   |
   v
DECISION
   |
   v
DELIVERY
   |
   v
OPERATIONS
   |
   v
EVIDENCE
   |
   v
INTELLIGENCE
   |
   v
IMPROVEMENT
   |
   +----------------+
                    |
                    v
                 STRATEGY
```

Architecture shall therefore remain connected to both strategic direction and operational reality.

---

# 903. Enterprise Architecture as an Operating Capability

EA-IMETA shall be treated as a capability consisting of:

People

Processes

Information

Methods

Governance

Technology

Intelligence

Services

The architecture capability shall enable the enterprise to:

Understand

Design

Decide

Transform

Govern

Measure

Learn

Evolve

AI Domain

---

# 941. Solution Architect

Solution Architects translate enterprise and domain architecture into solution-specific designs.

They shall ensure:

Architecture Alignment

Requirements Traceability

Technical Feasibility

Security

Operations

---

# 942. Business Architect

Business Architects focus on:

Business Model

Capabilities

Value Streams

Operating Model

Business Processes

Business Outcomes

---

# 943. Data Architect

Data Architects focus on:

Data Architecture

Information Architecture

Data Models

Data Products

Data Governance

Data Lineage

Data Quality

---

# 944. Security Architect

Security Architects focus on:

Identity

Trust

Access

Protection

Threats

Security Architecture

Resilience

---

# 945. Technology Architect

Technology Architects focus on:

Infrastructure

Platforms

Cloud

Networks

Runtime

Technology Standards

---

# 946. AI Architect

AI Architects focus on:

AI Use Cases

Models

AI Platforms

Data

Evaluation

AI Governance

AI Operations

---

# 947. Agent Architect

Agent Architects focus on:

Agent Architecture

Agent Identity

Agent Tools

Agent Authority

Agent Coordination

Agent Governance

Agent Operations

---

# 948. Architecture Analyst

Architecture Analysts support:

Architecture Data

Models

Documentation

Analysis

Repository

Metrics

---

# 949. Architecture Intelligence Specialist

Architecture Intelligence Specialists support:

Knowledge Graphs

Semantic Models

Architecture Analytics

AI

Predictive Analysis

Recommendation Systems

---

# 950. Architecture Role Accountability

Every material architecture object shall have an accountable owner.

Ownership shall not be assumed from technical responsibility alone.

---

# 951. Architecture Responsibility Model

Responsibilities shall be defined across:

Own

Approve

Design

Review

Implement

Operate

Measure

Improve

---

# 952. Architecture RACI

The enterprise may use a RACI model.

```text
R = Responsible
A = Accountable
C = Consulted
I = Informed
```

Each material architecture process shall identify the applicable roles.

---

# 953. Architecture Collaboration

Architecture shall operate as a collaborative capability.

Collaboration shall occur across:

Business

Technology

Security

Data

Operations

Risk

Transformation

Finance

Governance

---

# 954. Federated Architecture Model

Large enterprises may use a federated architecture model.

```text
ENTERPRISE ARCHITECTURE
          |
     +----+----+----+
     |    |    |    |
     v    v    v    v
 DOMAIN DOMAIN DOMAIN DOMAIN
 ARCH.   ARCH.  ARCH.  ARCH.
```

Enterprise architecture establishes common direction while domains retain appropriate autonomy.

---

# 955. Federated Architecture Principle

Federation shall balance:

Enterprise Consistency

Domain Autonomy

Reuse

Local Optimization

Governance

---

# 956. Centralized Architecture Model

Smaller enterprises may use a centralized architecture capability.

```text
ENTERPRISE
    |
    v
CENTRAL ARCHITECTURE
    |
    +---- Business
    +---- Data
    +---- Technology
    +---- Security
    +---- AI
```

---

# 957. Hybrid Architecture Model

A hybrid model may combine centralized and federated capabilities.

This is often appropriate where:

Enterprise standards must be common

while

Domain implementation requires flexibility.

---

# 958. Architecture Operating Cadence

Architecture shall operate on multiple time horizons.

### Strategic

Annual or multi-year.

### Tactical

Quarterly or monthly.

### Operational

Weekly or continuous.

### Intelligent

Continuous monitoring and analysis.

---

# 959. Strategic Architecture Cycle

The strategic cycle shall include:

Strategy Review

Architecture Review

Target State Review

Investment Review

Roadmap Review

---

# 960. Tactical Architecture Cycle

The tactical cycle shall include:

Portfolio Review

Architecture Decisions

Transformation Review

Risk Review

Technology Lifecycle Review

---

# 961. Operational Architecture Cycle

The operational cycle shall include:

Architecture Health

Drift

Exceptions

Incidents

Technology Changes

Architecture Data Quality

---

# 962. Continuous Intelligence Cycle

Architecture Intelligence shall continuously monitor:

Architecture

Operations

Risk

Technology

Security

AI

Agents

Business Signals

---

# 963. Architecture Meeting Model

Architecture governance meetings should be purpose-driven.

Possible forums:

Architecture Strategy Review

Architecture Board

Domain Architecture Review

Solution Review

Technology Review

AI Review

Security Review

Transformation Review

---

# 964. Architecture Decision Forum

A decision forum shall exist for material architecture decisions.

The forum shall focus on:

Decision

Evidence

Alternatives

Risk

Value

Authority

# 965. Architecture Review Forum

A review forum shall focus on:

Quality

Conformance

Dependencies

Security

Operational Readiness

---

# 966. Architecture Intelligence Forum

An intelligence forum may review:

Emerging Risks

Technology Trends

Architecture Drift

Predictions

Recommendations

Scenarios

---

# 967. Architecture Communication

Architecture communication shall adapt to audience.

Executives require:

Value

Risk

Strategy

Decision

Business Impact

Architects require:

Models

Dependencies

Standards

Trade-Offs

Engineers require:

Interfaces

Patterns

Requirements

Implementation Constraints

# 968. Architecture Visualization

EA-IMETA shall support multiple visualization levels.

Executive View

Enterprise View

Domain View

Solution View

Technical View

Operational View

Intelligence View

The architecture repository shall be the authoritative location for approved architecture information.

It shall contain:

Decisions

Principles

Standards

Building Blocks

Reference Architectures

Risks

Exceptions

Roadmaps

Draft

Under Review

Approved

Deprecated

Retired

# 971. Architecture Information Lifecycle

Create

Validate

Approve

Publish

Use

Monitor

Update

Archive

Retire

# 972. Architecture Data Stewardship

Architecture data shall have assigned stewardship.

Stewards shall monitor:

Completeness

Accuracy

Consistency

Timeliness

Ownership

---

# 973. Architecture Quality Management

Architecture quality shall be continuously assessed.

Quality dimensions include:

Completeness

Consistency

Traceability

Accuracy

Relevance

Usability

# 974. Architecture Quality Gates

Quality gates may apply to:

Architecture Models

Architecture Decisions

Reference Architectures

Building Blocks

Transformation Roadmaps

AI Architectures

Agent Architectures

---

# 975. Architecture Operating Metrics

The operating model shall measure:

Architecture Demand

Delivery Time

Decision Time

Review Time

Reuse

Compliance

Exceptions

Architecture Debt

Value

---

# 976. Architecture Service Levels

Architecture services may have defined service levels.

Examples:

Standard Review

Fast-Track Review

Strategic Review

Emergency Review

---

# 977. Fast-Track Architecture

Fast-track architecture may be used for:

Low-Risk

Low-Impact

Standardized

Reusable

Well-Understood

decisions.

# 978. Strategic Architecture Engagement

Strategic engagements shall address:

Major Business Change

Major Technology Change

Enterprise Transformation

Mergers

Acquisitions

Major AI Adoption

Major Platform Change

# 979. Architecture Innovation

Architecture shall support controlled innovation.

Innovation mechanisms may include:

Architecture Lab

Sandbox

Proof of Concept

Pilot

Reference Implementation

# 980. Innovation Governance

Innovation shall have:

Objective

Scope

Owner

Time Limit

Success Criteria

Transition Criteria

# 981. Architecture Research

Architecture research shall investigate:

Emerging Technology

AI

Agent Systems

Digital Twins

Autonomous Systems

New Security Models

```text
  v

  v

  v
EVALUATE

  |
```

# 983. Architecture Vendor Management

Architecture Fit

Lifecycle

Innovation

# 984. Architecture Technology Radar

The enterprise may maintain a technology radar with categories:

Retire

# 985. Architecture Technology Radar Governance

Technology radar decisions shall be based on:

Evidence

Strategic Relevance

Maturity

Risk

Architecture Fit

Owner

Scope

Version

Status

Exceptions

# 987. Architecture Pattern Management

Pattern ID

Purpose

Context

Problem

Solution

Consequences

Known Limitations

# 988. Architecture Building Block Management

Building Blocks shall be managed according to the framework established in Part 2.

The operating model shall ensure that approved Building Blocks are:

Discoverable

Reusable

Governed

Versioned

Measured

---

# 989. Architecture Knowledge Reuse

Architecture reuse shall be actively encouraged.

Reuse opportunities shall be identified through:

Search

Knowledge Graph

Architecture Intelligence

Reference Architectures

Patterns

Building Blocks

---

# 990. Architecture Decision Reuse

Previous decisions shall be reusable where:

Context is comparable

Assumptions remain valid

Risk remains acceptable

Architecture remains relevant

# 991. Architecture Operating Risk

The architecture function itself shall manage risks including:

Insufficient Capacity

Poor Data Quality

Weak Governance

Architecture Fragmentation

Skill Gaps

Technology Bias

Lack of Executive Sponsorship

# 992. Architecture Capability Risk Register

The architecture capability shall maintain risks covering:

People

Process

Information

Governance

Technology

Intelligence

Transformation

---

# 993. Architecture Continuity

The architecture capability shall remain resilient during:

Personnel Changes

Organizational Changes

Major Incidents

Transformation

Technology Changes

Leadership Changes

---

# 994. Architecture Institutional Memory

Architecture knowledge shall not depend solely on individual architects.

Institutional memory shall be maintained through:

Repository

Knowledge Graph

Decision Records

Patterns

Standards

Architecture Models

Lessons Learned

# 995. Architecture Onboarding

New architecture personnel shall receive:

Architecture Principles

Operating Model

Repository Access

Role Definition

Governance Model

Standards

Current State

Target State

# 996. Architecture Offboarding

Open Risks

Architecture Work

Stakeholders

Key Assumptions

Outstanding Exceptions

# 997. Architecture Professional Development

Architecture personnel shall maintain competence through:

Training

Research

Peer Review

Emerging Technology Evaluation

# 998. Architecture Community

It may share:

Patterns

Lessons

Standards

Architecture Decisions

Research

Emerging Technology

# 999. Architecture Operating Model Maturity

EA-IMETA operating maturity shall progress through:

LEVEL 1
AD HOC

LEVEL 2
DEFINED

LEVEL 3
STANDARDIZED

LEVEL 4
INTEGRATED

LEVEL 5
INTELLIGENT

LEVEL 6
ADAPTIVE

# 1000. Level 1 – Ad Hoc

Architecture activity depends primarily on individual expertise.

---

# 1001. Level 2 – Defined

Architecture roles and processes are documented.

---

# 1002. Level 3 – Standardized

Architecture services, governance, and methods are standardized.

---

# 1003. Level 4 – Integrated

Architecture is integrated with:

Strategy

Investment

Transformation

Operations

Governance

---

# 1004. Level 5 – Intelligent

Architecture Intelligence supports:

Search

Analysis

Prediction

Recommendations

Continuous Assurance

---

# 1005. Level 6 – Adaptive

The architecture capability continuously adapts according to:

Enterprise Change

Operational Evidence

Risk

Technology

Intelligence

Strategic Direction

```text
  v
```

```text
MASTER ARCHITECTURE
```

# 1008. Architecture as an Enterprise Service

EA-IMETA shall ultimately be understood as an enterprise service that provides:

Clarity

Alignment

Control

Learning

Evolution

# 1009. Architecture Value Proposition

The architecture capability shall create value by:

Reducing Complexity

Reducing Risk

Improving Decisions

Accelerating Transformation

# 1010. Architecture Operating Model Final Principle

The operating model shall ensure that architecture is:

AVAILABLE

WHEN NEEDED

GOVERNED

WHEN IMPORTANT

DETAILED

WHEN COMPLEX

INTELLIGENT

WHEN DATA IS AVAILABLE

ADAPTIVE

WHEN THE ENTERPRISE CHANGES.

---

# 1011. Section 1 Summary

EA-IMETA-MASTER-01 Part 3 Section 1 establishes the Enterprise Architecture Operating Model.

The section defines architecture as an active enterprise capability rather than a documentation function.

It establishes:

Architecture Strategy

Architecture Planning

Architecture Development

Architecture Governance

Architecture Assurance

Architecture Intelligence

Architecture Transformation

Architecture Operations

Architecture Knowledge Management

Architecture Performance Management

Architecture Evolution

It also establishes:

Architecture Services

Architecture Roles

Architecture Demand Management

Architecture Capacity

Architecture Collaboration

Federated Architecture

Architecture Cadence

Architecture Repository Operations

Architecture Quality

Architecture Innovation

Architecture Research

Architecture Professional Development

Architecture Maturity

The central principle is:

EA-IMETA SHALL OPERATE AS A CONTINUOUS
ENTERPRISE CAPABILITY THAT CONNECTS
STRATEGY, ARCHITECTURE, GOVERNANCE,
TRANSFORMATION, OPERATIONS, INTELLIGENCE,
AND EVOLUTION.

The resulting operating cycle is:

```text
DEMAND
  ↓
INTAKE
  ↓
PRIORITIZE
  ↓
DESIGN
  ↓
REVIEW
  ↓
DECIDE
  ↓
DELIVER
  ↓
OPERATE
  ↓
MEASURE
  ↓
LEARN
  ↓
IMPROVE
  ↓
DEMAND
```

This establishes the operational foundation for Part 3 of the EA-IMETA Master Architecture.

# 1012. EA-IMETA Architecture Lifecycle and Delivery Framework

## 1012.1 Purpose

EA-IMETA Part 3 Section 2 defines the Architecture Lifecycle and Delivery Framework.

The framework establishes how architecture work moves from initial demand through analysis, design, governance, implementation, operation, measurement, and eventual retirement.

The purpose is to create a consistent architecture delivery lifecycle that is:

Repeatable

Governed

Traceable

Risk-Based

Value-Oriented

Evidence-Based

Scalable

Adaptable

The lifecycle shall apply across:

Enterprise Architecture

Domain Architecture

Solution Architecture

Data Architecture

Technology Architecture

Security Architecture

AI Architecture

Agent Architecture

Transformation Architecture

---

# 1013. Architecture Lifecycle Principle

Architecture shall be treated as a lifecycle rather than a one-time deliverable.

The lifecycle is:

```text
DEMAND
  |
  v
DISCOVER
  |
  v
ASSESS
  |
  v
DEFINE
  |
  v
DESIGN
  |
  v
REVIEW
  |
  v
DECIDE
  |
  v
DELIVER
  |
  v
OPERATE
  |
  v
MEASURE
  |
  v
EVOLVE
  |
  v
RETIRE
```

Each stage shall generate information that supports the following stage.

---

# 1014. Architecture Lifecycle Objectives

The lifecycle shall ensure:

Clear Entry Criteria

Defined Outputs

Appropriate Governance

Decision Traceability

Risk Management

Stakeholder Participation

Architecture Quality

Operational Readiness

Value Measurement

Lifecycle Closure

---

# 1015. Architecture Lifecycle Scope

The lifecycle applies from:

Initial Idea

through:

Architecture Development

Implementation

Operation

Modernization

Retirement

The lifecycle therefore covers the complete architectural existence of a capability, solution, platform, or architecture component.

---

# 1016. Architecture Lifecycle States

Architecture objects shall have lifecycle states.

Recommended states are:

Proposed

Under Analysis

Under Design

Under Review

Approved

In Implementation

Operational

Under Modernization

Deprecated

Retired

---

# 1017. Lifecycle State Model

```text
PROPOSED
   |
   v
ANALYSIS
   |
   v
DESIGN
   |
   v
REVIEW
   |
   v
APPROVED
   |
   v
IMPLEMENTATION
   |
   v
OPERATION
   |
   v
MODERNIZATION
   |
   v
RETIREMENT
```

Not every architecture object must pass through every state in the same way.

---

# 1018. Architecture Demand

Architecture lifecycle activity normally begins with a demand.

Demand may originate from:

Strategy

Business

Technology

Security

Operations

Regulation

Risk

Transformation

Innovation

AI

Agent Requirements

---

# 1019. Architecture Demand Types

Demand shall be categorized where useful.

Examples:

Strategic Demand

Transformation Demand

Solution Demand

Technology Demand

Security Demand

Data Demand

AI Demand

Operational Demand

Regulatory Demand

Innovation Demand

---

# 1020. Architecture Intake

Architecture intake establishes the initial context.

The intake should capture:

Request ID

Requester

Business Need

Problem

Desired Outcome

Scope

Urgency

Affected Domain

Known Constraints

---

# 1021. Architecture Intake Quality

An architecture request should not be accepted as a fully formed architecture requirement merely because a request has been submitted.

The intake process shall distinguish between:

Problem

Need

Requirement

Solution

Decision

This prevents premature technology selection.

---

# 1022. Problem Definition

The first architecture question should be:

WHAT PROBLEM ARE WE TRYING TO SOLVE?

The architecture process shall avoid beginning with:

WHAT TECHNOLOGY SHOULD WE BUY?

---

# 1023. Business Outcome

The desired business outcome shall be identified where possible.

Examples:

Reduced Cost

Improved Customer Experience

Higher Resilience

Faster Processing

Improved Compliance

New Revenue

Reduced Risk

Improved Employee Experience

---

# 1024. Architecture Triage

Architecture triage determines the appropriate lifecycle path.

Triage shall consider:

Impact

Complexity

Risk

Strategic Importance

Urgency

Architecture Scope

---

# 1025. Architecture Triage Classes

Requests may be classified as:

### Simple

Low Risk and Standardized.

### Standard

Known Pattern or Building Block.

### Complex

Multiple Domains or Significant Dependencies.

### Strategic

Enterprise-Wide or High Strategic Impact.

### Critical

High Risk, Security, Regulatory, or Operational Impact.

---

# 1026. Lifecycle Path Selection

The lifecycle path shall be proportional to complexity.

```text
LOW RISK
   |
   v
FAST PATH

STANDARD
   |
   v
STANDARD PATH

COMPLEX
   |
   v
FULL ARCHITECTURE PATH

STRATEGIC / CRITICAL
   |
   v
ENHANCED GOVERNANCE PATH
```

---

# 1027. Architecture Discovery

Discovery establishes context before design.

Discovery shall consider:

Business Context

Stakeholders

Current Architecture

Capabilities

Processes

Data

Applications

Technology

Security

Operations

Dependencies

---

# 1028. Architecture Discovery Questions

Discovery should answer:

What exists?

What is changing?

Who is affected?

What depends on what?

What constraints exist?

What risks exist?

What is the desired outcome?

---

# 1029. Stakeholder Identification

Architecture shall identify stakeholders according to:

Decision Authority

Business Impact

Technical Impact

Operational Impact

Security Impact

Data Impact

Regulatory Impact

---

# 1030. Stakeholder Map

```text
DECISION MAKERS
      |
      v
BUSINESS STAKEHOLDERS
      |
      v
ARCHITECTS
      |
      v
ENGINEERING
      |
      v
SECURITY / DATA / OPERATIONS
      |
      v
USERS / CUSTOMERS
```

The actual stakeholder structure depends on context.

---

# 1031. Current State Assessment

The current state shall be assessed before defining major architectural change.

Assessment may include:

Capabilities

Processes

Applications

Data

Technology

Security

Operations

Costs

Risks

Technical Debt

---

# 1032. Current State Evidence

Current state architecture shall be supported by evidence.

Evidence may include:

Architecture Models

System Inventory

Operational Data

Configuration

Documentation

Logs

Monitoring

Interviews

---

# 1033. Architecture Baseline

The validated current state shall establish the baseline against which change is measured.

The baseline should be versioned.

---

# 1034. Architecture Requirements

Architecture requirements shall be derived from:

Business Requirements

Capability Needs

Security Requirements

Data Requirements

Regulatory Requirements

Operational Requirements

Technology Constraints

---

# 1035. Architecture Requirement Categories

Requirements may include:

Functional

Non-Functional

Security

Data

Performance

Availability

Resilience

Scalability

Compliance

Interoperability

Observability

AI Governance

Agent Governance

---

# 1036. Requirement Traceability

Architecture requirements shall be traceable to:

Business Need

Capability

Architecture Decision

Design

Implementation

Test

Outcome

---

# 1037. Traceability Model

```text
BUSINESS NEED
     |
     v
REQUIREMENT
     |
     v
ARCHITECTURE
     |
     v
DECISION
     |
     v
DESIGN
     |
     v
IMPLEMENTATION
     |
     v
TEST
     |
     v
OUTCOME
```

---

# 1038. Architecture Options

Architecture shall identify reasonable options before selecting a solution where material decisions are involved.

Options may include:

Build

Buy

Reuse

Extend

Replace

Modernize

Outsource

Platform

Cloud

Hybrid

---

# 1039. Option Evaluation

Options shall be evaluated using relevant criteria:

Business Fit

Architecture Fit

Cost

Risk

Security

Time

Complexity

Scalability

Resilience

Vendor Risk

---

# 1040. Architecture Trade-Off Analysis

Architecture decisions frequently require trade-offs.

Examples:

Cost versus Resilience

Speed versus Flexibility

Standardization versus Local Optimization

Automation versus Human Control

Innovation versus Stability

Performance versus Cost

---

# 1041. Architecture Decision Preparation

Before a material decision is made, the architecture team shall prepare:

Context

Problem

Options

Evaluation

Recommendation

Risks

Dependencies

Consequences

---

# 1042. Architecture Design

Architecture design converts requirements and decisions into an architecture model.

Design may include:

Capabilities

Processes

Services

Applications

Data

Integration

Technology

Security

AI

Agents

Operations

---

# 1043. Architecture Design Levels

Architecture design may be performed at:

Enterprise Level

Domain Level

Solution Level

Component Level

Implementation Level

---

# 1044. Architecture Abstraction

Architecture shall maintain appropriate abstraction.

Too much detail creates unnecessary complexity.

Too little detail prevents meaningful decisions.

The level of detail shall be proportional to:

Decision

Risk

Scope

Lifecycle

---

# 1045. Architecture Views

Architecture views may include:

Business View

Capability View

Process View

Information View

Data View

Application View

Integration View

Technology View

Security View

AI View

Agent View

Operational View

---

# 1046. Architecture Viewpoint

Each view shall have:

Purpose

Audience

Scope

Model

Notation

Owner

---

# 1047. Architecture Model Validation

Architecture models shall be validated for:

Completeness

Consistency

Traceability

Correctness

Stakeholder Relevance

---

# 1048. Architecture Review

The architecture review shall challenge:

Assumptions

Requirements

Options

Dependencies

Risks

Standards

Security

Operations

---

# 1049. Review Independence

Where appropriate, architecture reviews should be performed by someone other than the primary designer.

The degree of independence shall reflect:

Risk

Impact

Complexity

---

# 1050. Architecture Decision

The architecture decision shall identify:

Decision

Authority

Rationale

Evidence

Consequences

Risk

Review Date

---

# 1051. Architecture Approval

Approval shall confirm that:

Required Analysis Has Been Completed

Risks Are Known

Security Is Addressed

Dependencies Are Understood

The Architecture Is Acceptable

---

# 1052. Conditional Approval

Architecture may be approved conditionally where:

Minor Issues Remain

Known Mitigations Exist

Risks Are Accepted

Conditions Are Tracked

---

# 1053. Architecture Rejection

A proposal may be rejected where:

Strategic Alignment Is Insufficient

Risk Is Unacceptable

Security Is Insufficient

Cost Is Unjustified

Requirements Are Not Met

Better Alternatives Exist

---

# 1054. Architecture Implementation

After approval, architecture shall be translated into implementation work.

Implementation may include:

Projects

Products

Teams

Work Packages

Migration

Configuration

Development

Procurement

---

# 1055. Implementation Traceability

Implementation shall remain traceable to approved architecture.

```text
APPROVED ARCHITECTURE
        |
        v
IMPLEMENTATION PLAN
        |
        v
WORK PACKAGES
        |
        v
DELIVERY
        |
        v
VALIDATION
```

---

# 1056. Architecture Conformance During Delivery

Architecture conformance shall be assessed during implementation.

This prevents architecture from becoming irrelevant after approval.

---

# 1057. Architecture Change During Implementation

Implementation may reveal that approved architecture requires change.

Such changes shall follow the architecture change process.

Material deviations shall not be hidden within implementation.

---

# 1058. Implementation Exceptions

Where implementation cannot conform to approved architecture:

An exception shall be raised.

The exception shall identify:

Deviation

Reason

Risk

Impact

Mitigation

Owner

Duration

---

# 1059. Operational Readiness

Before operational acceptance, the architecture shall confirm:

Security

Monitoring

Support

Resilience

Backup

Recovery

Capacity

Documentation

Ownership

---

# 1060. Architecture Operational Handover

Operational handover shall include:

Architecture Model

Dependencies

Interfaces

Technology

Security

Support Model

Operational Requirements

Known Risks

Known Limitations

---

# 1061. Architecture in Operation

Once operational, architecture shall remain monitored.

Monitoring shall consider:

Availability

Performance

Security

Cost

Capacity

Dependencies

Architecture Drift

Lifecycle

---

# 1062. Architecture Health Monitoring

Architecture health shall provide an ongoing assessment of:

Business Alignment

Technical Health

Security

Risk

Operational Health

Data Quality

Architecture Debt

---

# 1063. Architecture Drift

Operational implementation may diverge from approved architecture.

Drift shall be:

Detected

Classified

Assessed

Accepted

Remediated

or

Formally Approved as an Exception.

---

# 1064. Architecture Lifecycle Evidence

Evidence may originate from:

Monitoring

Incidents

Changes

Projects

Security

Audits

Operational Metrics

Business Performance

---

# 1065. Architecture Measurement

Architecture measurement shall evaluate:

Performance

Value

Risk

Compliance

Quality

Transformation

---

# 1066. Architecture Benefits

Benefits shall be measured against the original objectives.

Examples:

Cost Reduction

Time Reduction

Risk Reduction

Revenue

Quality

Resilience

Customer Experience

---

# 1067. Post-Implementation Review

The post-implementation review shall determine:

Was the architecture implemented?

Did the solution achieve the intended outcome?

Were assumptions correct?

What changed?

What risks remain?

What should be learned?

---

# 1068. Architecture Lessons Learned

Lessons shall be captured from:

Decisions

Implementation

Incidents

Exceptions

Operational Performance

User Feedback

---

# 1069. Architecture Knowledge Update

Lessons learned shall update:

Patterns

Standards

Building Blocks

Reference Architectures

Principles

Governance

---

# 1070. Architecture Modernization Trigger

Modernization may be triggered by:

Technology Obsolescence

Security Risk

Performance

Cost

Business Change

Architecture Debt

Vendor Exit

Regulatory Change

---

# 1071. Architecture Modernization Assessment

The assessment shall compare:

Current Condition

Business Need

Target Capability

Technology Options

Risk

Cost

---

# 1072. Architecture Modernization Decision

Modernization options may include:

Maintain

Optimize

Refactor

Replatform

Replace

Consolidate

Retire

---

# 1073. Architecture Retirement

Retirement shall be considered when:

Capability Is No Longer Required

Technology Is Obsolete

Cost Is Excessive

Risk Is Excessive

Replacement Exists

---

# 1074. Retirement Assessment

Retirement shall assess:

Dependencies

Data

Users

Contracts

Security

Migration

Operational Impact

---

# 1075. Architecture Retirement Validation

After retirement:

Access shall be removed

Dependencies shall be verified

Data retention shall be confirmed

Monitoring shall be updated

Architecture records shall be updated

---

# 1076. Architecture Lifecycle Governance

Lifecycle governance shall ensure:

Correct Stage

Correct Authority

Correct Evidence

Correct Decision

Correct Documentation

---

# 1077. Lifecycle Governance Matrix

| Lifecycle Stage | Primary Objective | Typical Authority |
|---|---|---|
| Demand | Establish need | Business / Architecture |
| Discovery | Understand context | Architecture |
| Assessment | Evaluate condition | Architecture |
| Definition | Establish requirements | Business / Architecture |
| Design | Create architecture | Architecture |
| Review | Challenge design | Architecture Governance |
| Decision | Approve direction | Authorized Authority |
| Delivery | Implement | Delivery Organization |
| Operation | Run capability | Operations |
| Measure | Assess outcomes | Business / Architecture |
| Evolve | Improve | Architecture / Governance |
| Retire | End lifecycle | Business / Architecture |

---

# 1078. Lifecycle Governance Proportionality

Governance shall be proportional to:

Risk

Impact

Cost

Complexity

Irreversibility

Strategic Importance

---

# 1079. Architecture Fast Path

The fast path may be used when:

Pattern Is Known

Building Block Is Approved

Risk Is Low

Impact Is Limited

Requirements Are Clear

---

# 1080. Architecture Full Path

The full path shall be used when:

Multiple Domains Are Affected

Architecture Is New

Risk Is Significant

Dependencies Are Complex

Strategic Impact Is High

---

# 1081. Architecture Critical Path

Critical architecture shall receive enhanced governance where it affects:

Safety

Critical Services

Security

Regulation

Major Financial Exposure

Enterprise Resilience

---

Metrics

# 1105. Lifecycle Improvement

Lifecycle performance shall be continuously improved.

# 1107. Level 1 – Documented

The lifecycle exists as documented guidance.

---

# 1108. Level 2 – Repeatable

The lifecycle is consistently applied.

---

# 1109. Level 3 – Governed

---

# 1110. Level 4 – Measured

# 1111. Level 5 – Intelligent

Planning

Validation

Benefits

Architecture delivery may be divided into:

Design Package

Implementation Package

Migration Package

Security Package

Data Package

Integration Package

Operational Package

---

# 1117. Architecture Delivery Dependencies

Delivery dependencies shall be explicitly managed.

Examples:

Security before Deployment

Data Migration before Cutover

Integration before Service Activation

Operations before Production

Training before User Adoption

---

# 1118. Delivery Readiness

Before delivery begins, readiness should be assessed for:

Architecture

Requirements

People

Technology

Data

Security

Operations

Governance

# 1119. Delivery Acceptance

Acceptance shall verify:

Requirements

Architecture

Quality

Security

Operations

Benefits

---

Evidence shall include:

Design

Implementation

Tests

Security Assessments

Operational Readiness

Approval

---

# 1121. Architecture Delivery Quality

Delivery quality shall assess:

Conformance

Completeness

Performance

Security

Resilience

Maintainability

---

# 1122. Architecture Delivery Risk

Delivery risk shall consider:

Technical

Schedule

Cost

Dependency

Security

Data

People

Vendor

---

# 1123. Architecture Delivery Escalation

Escalation shall occur when:

Architecture Deviates

Risk Increases

Benefits Decline

Dependencies Fail

Security Changes

Scope Changes

---

# 1124. Architecture Delivery Closure

A delivery shall not be considered architecturally complete until:

Implementation Is Validated

Operational Ownership Exists

Documentation Is Updated

Architecture Repository Is Updated

Risks Are Recorded

Lessons Are Captured

---

# 1125. Architecture Lifecycle Closure

Lifecycle closure shall occur when:

The capability is retired

or

Ownership is formally transferred

or

The architecture object is replaced.

---

# 1126. Section 2 Summary

EA-IMETA-MASTER-01 Part 3 Section 2 establishes the Architecture Lifecycle and Delivery Framework.

The framework defines the complete lifecycle from:

DEMAND

to:

DISCOVERY

ASSESSMENT

DEFINITION

DESIGN

REVIEW

DECISION

DELIVERY

OPERATION

MEASUREMENT

EVOLUTION

RETIREMENT

The framework establishes:

Architecture Intake

Triage

Discovery

Current State

Requirements

Option Analysis

Architecture Design

Review

Approval

Implementation

Operational Readiness

Conformance

Architecture Health

Modernization

Retirement

Lifecycle Governance

Lifecycle Automation

Architecture Versioning

Configuration Management

Security Lifecycle

Data Lifecycle

AI Lifecycle

Agent Lifecycle

Delivery Management

Lifecycle Maturity

The central principle is:

ARCHITECTURE SHALL BE MANAGED AS A
COMPLETE, GOVERNED, TRACEABLE, AND
CONTINUOUSLY IMPROVING LIFECYCLE.

The operating lifecycle is:

```text
DEMAND
  ↓
DISCOVER
  ↓
ASSESS
  ↓
DEFINE
  ↓
DESIGN
  ↓
REVIEW
  ↓
DECIDE
  ↓
DELIVER
  ↓
OPERATE
  ↓
MEASURE
  ↓
LEARN
  ↓
EVOLVE
  ↓
RETIRE
```

This establishes the lifecycle and delivery foundation for the remaining sections of Part 3.

# 1126. EA-IMETA Architecture Portfolio and Capability Management Framework

## 1126.1 Purpose

EA-IMETA Part 3 Section 3 establishes the Architecture Portfolio and Capability Management Framework.

The framework defines how the enterprise identifies, models, assesses, prioritizes, develops, governs, measures, and evolves the capabilities required to achieve strategic objectives.

The framework connects:

Strategy

Business Capabilities

Architecture

Investment

Transformation

Applications

Technology

Data

AI

Agents

People

Operations

Value

The objective is to ensure that enterprise architecture remains centered on what the enterprise must be capable of doing rather than on technology components alone.

---

# 1127. Capability Principle

A capability describes what an enterprise is able to do.

A capability is therefore distinct from:

A Process

An Organization

An Application

A Technology

A Project

A Product

A Person

The capability abstraction shall remain sufficiently stable to support strategic planning while implementation may change over time.

---

# 1128. Capability-Based Architecture

The architecture shall use capabilities as a central bridge between strategy and implementation.

The relationship is:

```text
STRATEGY
   |
   v
STRATEGIC OBJECTIVES
   |
   v
CAPABILITIES
   |
   v
VALUE STREAMS
   |
   v
SERVICES / PROCESSES
   |
   v
APPLICATIONS / DATA / TECHNOLOGY
   |
   v
OUTCOMES
```

---

# 1129. Capability Definition

Each material capability shall have:

Capability ID

Name

Purpose

Description

Owner

Business Domain

Strategic Alignment

Maturity

Importance

Dependencies

Supporting Architecture

Performance

Risk

Lifecycle State

---

# 1130. Capability Hierarchy

Capabilities may be organized hierarchically.

```text
ENTERPRISE CAPABILITY
        |
        +---- DOMAIN CAPABILITY
        |          |
        |          +---- SUB-CAPABILITY
        |
        +---- DOMAIN CAPABILITY
                   |
                   +---- SUB-CAPABILITY
```

The hierarchy shall remain understandable and useful for decision making.

---

# 1131. Capability Levels

Capability models may use multiple levels.

Typical levels are:

Level 0 – Enterprise

Level 1 – Domain

Level 2 – Capability

Level 3 – Sub-Capability

Level 4 – Detailed Capability

The exact number of levels shall depend on enterprise complexity.

---

# 1132. Capability Ownership

Every material capability shall have an accountable owner.

The owner is responsible for:

Capability Direction

Performance

Maturity

Investment

Risk

Transformation

Architecture Alignment

---

# 1133. Capability Characteristics

Capabilities shall be:

Outcome Oriented

Stable Relative to Processes

Business Relevant

Measurable

Owned

Traceable

Composable

---

# 1134. Capability versus Process

The architecture shall distinguish:

CAPABILITY

What the enterprise can do.

PROCESS

How work is performed.

The same capability may be implemented through multiple processes.

---

# 1135. Capability versus Organization

A capability shall not be equated directly with an organizational unit.

One organization may provide multiple capabilities.

One capability may span multiple organizations.

---

# 1136. Capability versus Application

Applications support capabilities.

They do not define the capability itself.

This distinction supports technology-independent strategic planning.

---

# 1137. Capability versus Technology

Technology enables capability.

Technology shall not become the definition of the capability.

---

# 1138. Capability Map

The enterprise shall maintain a capability map.

The capability map shall provide a structured overview of:

Capability Areas

Capabilities

Sub-Capabilities

Strategic Importance

Maturity

Performance

Risk

---

# 1139. Capability Map Example

```text
ENTERPRISE
 |
 +-- CUSTOMER
 |    +-- Customer Management
 |    +-- Customer Service
 |    +-- Customer Insight
 |
 +-- OPERATIONS
 |    +-- Planning
 |    +-- Execution
 |    +-- Monitoring
 |
 +-- FINANCE
 |    +-- Financial Management
 |    +-- Accounting
 |    +-- Reporting
 |
 +-- PEOPLE
      +-- Workforce Management
      +-- Skills Management
```

This is illustrative and shall be adapted to enterprise context.

---

# 1140. Capability Assessment

Capabilities shall be assessed according to:

Importance

Maturity

Performance

Risk

Strategic Alignment

Investment

Technology Support

Data Support

People Capability

---

# 1141. Capability Maturity

EA-IMETA shall support a capability maturity scale.

Recommended levels:

LEVEL 1
INITIAL

LEVEL 2
DEVELOPING

LEVEL 3
DEFINED

LEVEL 4
MANAGED

LEVEL 5
OPTIMIZED

LEVEL 6
INTELLIGENT / ADAPTIVE

---

# 1142. Level 1 – Initial Capability

The capability exists but is:

Informal

Inconsistent

Dependent on Individuals

Poorly Measured

---

# 1143. Level 2 – Developing Capability

The capability has:

Defined Ownership

Emerging Processes

Basic Measurement

Partial Governance

---

# 1144. Level 3 – Defined Capability

The capability has:

Defined Processes

Defined Roles

Defined Information

Defined Services

Defined Standards

---

# 1145. Level 4 – Managed Capability

The capability is:

Measured

Governed

Integrated

Predictable

---

# 1146. Level 5 – Optimized Capability

The capability is continuously improved using:

Performance Data

Automation

Optimization

Benchmarking

---

# 1147. Level 6 – Intelligent / Adaptive Capability

The capability uses:

AI

Automation

Architecture Intelligence

Predictive Analytics

Adaptive Operations

where appropriate.

---

# 1148. Capability Heatmap

The capability portfolio may use a heatmap showing:

Strategic Importance

Current Maturity

Target Maturity

Risk

Investment

---

# 1149. Capability Heatmap Model

```text
                    STRATEGIC IMPORTANCE
                 LOW       MEDIUM       HIGH

MATURITY
HIGH             Maintain   Optimize    Protect
MEDIUM           Improve    Transform   Accelerate
LOW              Monitor    Develop     PRIORITIZE
```

The exact classification shall be enterprise specific.

---

# 1150. Capability Gap Analysis

Capability gap analysis compares:

Current Capability

Required Capability

Target Capability

The resulting gap may be:

Minor

Moderate

Significant

Critical

# 1151. Capability Gap Model

```text
CURRENT CAPABILITY
        |
        v
CAPABILITY GAP
        |
        v
TARGET CAPABILITY
        |
        v
TRANSFORMATION
```

---

# 1152. Capability Investment

Capability investment shall consider:

Strategic Importance

Business Value

Maturity Gap

Risk

Cost

Dependencies

Time to Value

---

# 1153. Capability Investment Categories

Capabilities may be categorized as:

Invest

Improve

Protect

Transform

Maintain

Consolidate

Retire

---

# 1154. Capability Dependency

Capabilities may depend on:

Other Capabilities

Processes

Services

Data

Applications

Technology

People

Partners

---

# 1155. Capability Dependency Graph

```text
CAPABILITY A
    |
    +---- requires ---> CAPABILITY B
    |
    +---- requires ---> DATA C
    |
    +---- supported by ---> APPLICATION D
    |
    +---- enabled by ---> TECHNOLOGY E
```

---

# 1156. Capability Criticality

Capability criticality shall consider:

Business Impact

Customer Impact

Financial Impact

Regulatory Impact

Operational Impact

Safety

Security

---

# 1157. Critical Capability

A critical capability is one where significant degradation or loss would materially affect enterprise objectives or essential services.

Critical capabilities shall receive enhanced architecture and resilience attention.

---

# 1158. Capability Resilience

Capability resilience shall consider:

Alternative Processes

Alternative Technology

People Redundancy

Data Availability

Service Continuity

Recovery

Supplier Dependency

---

# 1159. Capability Ownership Model

Capability ownership shall connect:

Business Owner

Architecture Owner

Process Owner

Technology Owner

Data Owner

Operational Owner

where relevant.

---

# 1160. Capability and Value Streams

Capabilities shall be connected to value streams.

```text
VALUE STREAM
      |
      +---- Capability A
      |
      +---- Capability B
      |
      +---- Capability C
      |
      v
BUSINESS OUTCOME
```

---

# 1161. Capability and Business Outcomes

The enterprise shall identify which outcomes depend on each major capability.

This supports investment prioritization.

---

# 1162. Capability and Strategic Objectives

Capabilities shall be traceable to strategic objectives.

```text
STRATEGIC OBJECTIVE
       |
       v
CAPABILITY
       |
       v
INITIATIVE
       |
       v
OUTCOME
```

---

# 1163. Capability Traceability

Capability traceability shall connect:

Strategy

Objective

Capability

Process

Service

Application

Data

Technology

Initiative

Outcome

Value

---

# 1164. Capability Portfolio

The capability portfolio shall provide a consolidated view of:

Current State

Target State

Maturity

Investment

Risk

Dependencies

Transformation

---

# 1165. Capability Portfolio Lifecycle

Capabilities may move through:

Emerging

Developing

Established

Strategic

Optimized

Declining

Retiring

---

# 1166. Emerging Capability

Emerging capabilities may arise from:

New Markets

New Technology

AI

New Regulations

Customer Demand

Business Model Change

---

# 1167. Strategic Capability

Strategic capabilities directly support critical strategic objectives.

They shall receive appropriate:

Investment

Governance

Architecture Attention

Risk Management

---

# 1168. Declining Capability

A declining capability may result from:

Business Model Change

Technology Change

Market Change

Consolidation

Outsourcing

Automation

---

# 1169. Capability Retirement

Capability retirement shall occur only when:

The capability is no longer required

or

The capability has been replaced

or

The business model has changed.

---

# 1170. Capability Lifecycle Model

```text
EMERGING
   |
   v
DEVELOPING
   |
   v
ESTABLISHED
   |
   v
STRATEGIC
   |
   v
OPTIMIZED
   |
   v
DECLINING
   |
   v
RETIRING
```

The lifecycle is not necessarily linear.

---

# 1171. Capability Architecture

Capability architecture shall provide a stable reference for:

Business Planning

Transformation

Investment

Technology Planning

Operating Model Design

---

# 1172. Capability-Based Transformation

Transformation initiatives shall be linked to capabilities.

Each initiative should identify:

Capabilities Changed

Current Maturity

Target Maturity

Expected Outcome

Investment

# 1173. Capability Transformation Model

```text
CURRENT CAPABILITY
       |
       v
TARGET CAPABILITY
       |
       v
GAP
       |
       v
TRANSFORMATION INITIATIVE
       |
       v
IMPLEMENTATION
       |
       v
NEW CAPABILITY STATE
```

---

# 1174. Capability Roadmap

The capability roadmap shall show:

Current Capability

Target Capability

Transformation Initiatives

Milestones

Dependencies

Benefits

# 1175. Capability Roadmap Example

```text
CURRENT
   |
   +---- Initiative A
   |
   v
MATURITY 2
   |
   +---- Initiative B
   |
   v
MATURITY 3
   |
   +---- Initiative C
   |
   v
TARGET MATURITY 5
```

---

# 1176. Capability Performance

Capability performance shall be measured through relevant indicators.

Possible indicators include:

Cost

Quality

Speed

Availability

Capacity

Customer Satisfaction

Risk

Compliance

---

# 1177. Capability KPIs

Each critical capability should have:

KPI

Baseline

Target

Owner

Measurement Frequency

Threshold

Escalation

---

# 1178. Capability Risk

Capability risks may include:

People Risk

Process Risk

Technology Risk

Data Risk

Security Risk

Supplier Risk

Regulatory Risk

---

# 1179. Capability Risk Heatmap

Impact

Criticality

```text
CAPABILITY
   |
   +---- PEOPLE
   |
   +---- PROCESS
   |
   +---- DATA
   |
   +---- APPLICATION
   |
   +---- TECHNOLOGY
   |
   +---- GOVERNANCE
```

A capability is therefore a composite enterprise construct.

Projects

# 1199. Total Lifecycle Cost

Portfolio decisions shall use lifecycle cost rather than acquisition cost alone.

```text
ACQUIRE
  +
IMPLEMENT
  +
OPERATE
  +
MAINTAIN
  +
SECURE
  +
CHANGE
  +
MIGRATE
  +
RETIRE
  =
TOTAL LIFECYCLE COST
```

# 1302. Section 3 Summary

EA-IMETA-MASTER-01 Part 3 Section 3 establishes the Architecture Portfolio and Capability Management Framework.

The framework places capabilities at the center of the relationship between:

Strategy

Business

Architecture

Investment

Transformation

Technology

Data

AI

Agents

Operations

Value

It establishes:

Capability Architecture

Capability Maps

Capability Ownership

Capability Maturity

Capability Gap Analysis

Capability Investment

Capability Risk

Capability Resilience

Capability Lifecycle

Capability Transformation

Capability Performance

Architecture Portfolio

Application Portfolio

Technology Portfolio

Data Portfolio

AI Portfolio

Agent Portfolio

Integration Portfolio

Portfolio Rationalization

Portfolio Financial Management

Portfolio Risk

Portfolio Resilience

Portfolio Optimization

Portfolio Intelligence

The central principle is:

ARCHITECTURE SHALL FOCUS ON WHAT THE
ENTERPRISE MUST BE ABLE TO DO, WHILE
ALLOWING THE TECHNOLOGY AND
IMPLEMENTATION USED TO ACHIEVE IT
TO EVOLVE.

The integrated operating cycle is:

```text
STRATEGY
  ↓
CAPABILITY
  ↓
PORTFOLIO
  ↓
ASSESS
  ↓
PRIORITIZE
  ↓
INVEST
  ↓
TRANSFORM
  ↓
OPERATE
  ↓
MEASURE
  ↓
LEARN
  ↓
ADAPT
  ↓
STRATEGY
```

This establishes the capability and portfolio foundation for the remaining sections of Part 3.

# 1303. EA-IMETA Architecture Information, Data and Knowledge Management Framework

## 1303.1 Purpose

EA-IMETA Part 3 Section 4 establishes the Architecture Information, Data and Knowledge Management Framework.

The framework defines how architecture information is created, structured, governed, connected, stored, maintained, discovered, analyzed, shared, protected, and ultimately retired.

The framework recognizes that enterprise architecture is fundamentally an information-intensive discipline.

The architecture capability depends on reliable information concerning:

Strategy

Capabilities

Processes

Services

Applications

Data

Technology

Infrastructure

Security

AI

Agents

People

Organizations

Risks

Decisions

Dependencies

Transformation

Operations

Benefits

The objective is to establish architecture information as a trusted enterprise asset.

---

# 1304. Architecture Information Principle

Architecture information shall be:

Accurate

Complete

Relevant

Traceable

Current

Owned

Governed

Accessible

Secure

Reusable

Machine-Readable Where Appropriate

Architecture information shall support both human decision making and machine-assisted architecture intelligence.

---

# 1305. Architecture Information as an Enterprise Asset

Architecture information shall be treated as an enterprise asset because it enables:

Understanding

Decision Making

Planning

Governance

Transformation

Risk Management

Optimization

Learning

---

# 1306. Architecture Information Domains

The architecture information environment shall contain information relating to:

Business

Capabilities

Processes

Services

Applications

Data

Technology

Security

AI

Agents

Organizations

People

Partners

Projects

Products

Risks

Decisions

Standards

Policies

Roadmaps

---

# 1307. Architecture Information Lifecycle

Architecture information shall follow a controlled lifecycle:

```text
CREATE
   |
   v
CAPTURE
   |
   v
VALIDATE
   |
   v
CLASSIFY
   |
   v
STORE
   |
   v
CONNECT
   |
   v
USE
   |
   v
UPDATE
   |
   v
ARCHIVE
   |
   v
RETIRE
```

---

# 1308. Information Creation

Architecture information may be created through:

Architecture Work

Projects

Transformation

Operations

Discovery

Interviews

Modeling

Monitoring

Security

Audits

AI Analysis

Agent Analysis

# 1309. Information Capture

Information shall be captured in a structured manner wherever practical.

Models

Repositories

APIs

Integration

Automated Discovery

Manual Entry

# 1310. Information Validation

Architecture information shall be validated for:

Completeness

Consistency

Ownership

Source

Recency

Confidence

# 1311. Information Classification

Architecture information shall be classified according to:

Type

Sensitivity

Authority

Lifecycle

Confidence

Security Classification

# 1312. Information Authority

Authoritative

Approved

Reference

Observed

Inferred

Draft

Unverified

Historical

# 1313. Information Confidence

Confidence may be based on:

Source Quality

Evidence

Recency

Consistency

Validation

Completeness

# 1314. Information Provenance

System

Date

Approval

Governs

Precedes

```text
    |
    v

    |
```

Type

Name

Description

Owner

Domain

Status

Lifecycle

# 1319. Architecture Identity

Each material architecture object shall have a stable identifier.

The identifier shall support:

Traceability

Versioning

Integration

History

Cross-Reference

# 1320. Identifier Principle

Identifiers shall remain stable even if:

Names Change

Technology Changes

Ownership Changes

Implementation Changes

This supports historical traceability.

# 1321. Architecture Naming

Architecture naming shall be:

Consistent

Understandable

Unambiguous

Stable

Searchable

---

# 1322. Naming Standards

Naming standards shall define conventions for:

Capabilities

Applications

Services

Data

Technology

Interfaces

Architecture Decisions

Risks

Initiatives

---

# 1323. Architecture Taxonomy

EA-IMETA shall maintain controlled taxonomies for:

Object Types

Domains

Lifecycle States

Risk Classes

Technology Categories

Capability Classes

Architecture Patterns

---

# 1324. Taxonomy Governance

Taxonomies shall have:

Owner

Version

Definition

Scope

Review Cycle

Change Process

---

# 1325. Controlled Vocabulary

Where practical, controlled vocabulary shall be used to reduce ambiguity.

Examples:

Lifecycle State

Risk Level

Architecture Status

Decision Type

Technology Category

---

# 1326. Architecture Ontology

The architecture ontology shall define the meaning of important architecture concepts and their relationships.

It shall support semantic interoperability between:

Architecture Repository

Knowledge Graph

Analytics

AI

Automation

---

# 1327. Ontology Principle

The ontology shall describe meaning rather than merely storing labels.

For example:

An Application

supports

a Capability

and

consumes

a Data Asset.

---

# 1328. Semantic Architecture

Semantic architecture shall allow systems to understand relationships between architecture objects.

This supports:

Impact Analysis

Reasoning

Search

Recommendation

Automation

---

# 1329. Architecture Knowledge Graph

The Knowledge Graph shall represent architecture relationships.

Example:

```text
[STRATEGY]
    |
    v
[OBJECTIVE]
    |
    v
[CAPABILITY]
    |
    +---- [PROCESS]
    |
    +---- [SERVICE]
    |
    +---- [APPLICATION]
    |
    +---- [DATA]
    |
    +---- [TECHNOLOGY]
    |
    +---- [RISK]
    |
    +---- [INITIATIVE]
    |
    +---- [OUTCOME]
```

# 1330. Knowledge Graph Purpose

The Knowledge Graph shall support:

Discovery

Relationship Analysis

Impact Analysis

Dependency Analysis

Traceability

Scenario Analysis

AI Reasoning

# 1331. Knowledge Graph Sources

Security Systems

# 1332. Knowledge Graph Reconciliation

Source A

Source B

Conflict

Confidence

Required Validation

```text
ARCHITECTURE MODEL
        |
        v

        |
        v

        |
        v
DIFFERENCE
        |
        v
VALIDATE
        |
        v
UPDATE
```

Architecture data quality dimensions shall include:

Accuracy

Completeness

Consistency

Timeliness

Uniqueness

Validity

Traceability

---

# 1335. Architecture Data Quality Score

A configurable data quality score may be calculated from:

Completeness

Accuracy

Consistency

Recency

Ownership

Validation

# 1336. Data Quality Thresholds

Architecture objects may require minimum data quality before being used for:

Critical Decisions

Automated Recommendations

Compliance

Executive Reporting

# 1337. Architecture Data Stewardship

Data stewardship shall ensure that architecture information remains usable.

Stewards shall monitor:

Quality

Ownership

Lifecycle

Classification

Consistency

---

# 1338. Architecture Data Ownership

Each critical information object shall have:

Business Owner

Data Steward

Architecture Owner

Technical Custodian

where appropriate.

# 1339. Architecture Repository

The architecture repository shall provide controlled storage for architecture information.

It shall contain:

Models

Objects

Relationships

Decisions

Principles

Standards

Patterns

Building Blocks

Roadmaps

Risks

Exceptions

---

# 1340. Repository Authority

The repository shall distinguish:

Source of Record

Reference Source

Temporary Source

External Source

Observed Source

# 1341. Repository Structure

A logical repository structure may include:

```text
EA-IMETA REPOSITORY
 |
 +-- Strategy
 +-- Business
 +-- Capabilities
 +-- Processes
 +-- Services
 +-- Applications
 +-- Data
 +-- Technology
 +-- Security
 +-- AI
 +-- Agents
 +-- Organizations
 +-- Decisions
 +-- Risks
 +-- Initiatives

 +-- Patterns
 +-- Roadmaps
```

Object Type

Owner

Domain

Lifecycle

Relationship

Technology

Capability

Risk

Decision
